# Almost-Linear-Time Maximum-Cardinality Matching in General Graphs

Coordinate `EN-114-000-0007-2-2026-PR-T4-UK-EX-M1-C-AL-H-0256` | `PM-120.1` | status ACTIVE | original slug `Almost-Linear-Time-Maximum-Cardinality-Matching-in-Sparse-General-Graphs-September-24-2026` | date 2026-09-24 | derived from the openai/math preprint Almost-Linear-Time-Maximum-Cardinality-Matching-in-Sparse-General-Graphs-September-24-2026, modified (Apache-2.0). Publication status: DRAFT.

<!-- PM:BEGIN pm.node.v2 -->
```json
{
  "abstract_verbatim": "We prove that maximum-cardinality matching in a simple undirected graph with <i>n</i> vertices and <i>m</i> edges can be found by one uniform randomized algorithm in $`(n+m)^{1+o(1)}`$ time. The bound holds on every computation path in a logarithmic-word model, and the algorithm returns an explicit maximum matching with probability at least 2/3. An explicit reduction gives the same time and probability guarantees for deciding whether a simple host graph has a spanning subgraph with prescribed valid vertex degrees, and for finding one when it exists.",

<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove that maximum-cardinality matching in a simple undirected graph with <i>n</i> vertices and <i>m</i> edges can be found by one uniform randomized algorithm in $`(n+m)^{1+o(1)}`$ time. The bound holds on every computation path in a logarithmic-word model, and the algorithm returns an explicit maximum matching with probability at least 2/3. An explicit reduction gives the same time and probability guarantees for deciding whether a simple host graph has a spanning subgraph with prescribed valid vertex degrees, and for finding one when it exists.

- **claim-uniform-maximum-matching** (algorithm, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): There are a single uniform randomized word-RAM algorithm, fixed constants $c_0\ge2$ and $C\ge1$, and a nonincreasing function $\eta:\mathbb N_{\ge1}\to\mathbb R_{\ge0}$ with $\eta(q)\to0$ as $q\to\infty$ such that the following holds. For every input graph $G=([n],E)$ with $m=\|E\|$ and $p=n+m$, the algorithm halts on every computation path in at most $$C p^{1+\eta(p)}$$ instructions. With probability at least $2/3$ it outputs an explicit list of edges of a matching of size $\nu(G)$.
  - Explanation: A single finite randomized program constructs an explicit maximum-cardinality matching in almost-linear adjacency-list input time. The instruction bound holds on unsuccessful random paths as well as successful ones.
- **claim-static-integral-flow** (algorithm, [source](#stmt-lem-integral-flow)): For an explicitly represented directed network of total size at most $P$, with integral capacities, demands and costs bounded in magnitude by a fixed power of $P$, exact integral minimum-cost flow can be computed uniformly and deterministically in $P^{1+o(1)}$ time. Its finite-precision operations can be simulated with polynomially logarithmic overhead on logarithmic-bit words. In particular, integral source–sink maximum flow with nonnegative upper capacities and zero lower capacities returns an explicit integral arc vector and a residual-reachability minimum-cut certificate within that bound.
  - Explanation: The static deterministic flow interface includes exact integral output, charged finite-precision simulation, and a residual minimum-cut certificate for its maximum-flow specialization.
- **claim-integral-small-violation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prelim-small-violation)): Let every lower and upper arc bound be integral, with lower bound no larger than upper bound. If an exactly conserved real arc vector has total bound violation less than one, the bounds admit an exactly feasible integral circulation.
  - Explanation: Exact conservation and a strict total-violation margin force all integral circulation cut inequalities to hold. This proves integral feasibility without claiming that the supplied real vector is itself integral.
- **claim-binary-capacity-scaling** (algorithm, [source](#stmt-lem-capacity-scaling)): For maximum flow with nonnegative integral upper capacities of at most $b$ bits, a single fixed polynomial-capacity version of Lemma <a href="#lem:integral-flow" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:integral-flow">2</a> suffices, using $b$ calls on linear-size residual graphs, together with the charged capacity arithmetic and identifier translations.
  - Explanation: Binary capacity rounds reduce large-capacity maximum flow to the same fixed polynomial-capacity implementation. Residual truncation preserves the required augmentation, while capacity arithmetic and identifier conversion remain charged.
- **claim-clocked-perfect-matching-fallback** (algorithm, [source](#stmt-lem-fallback)): There is one uniform deterministic algorithm on explicit loopless graphs of maximum degree three that halts within a fixed polynomial number of finite-word instructions. It returns a checked perfect matching when one exists and a negative answer otherwise.
  - Explanation: A uniform deterministic perfect-matching procedure supplies a polynomial clock and exact answers on loopless subcubic instances, including instances without a perfect matching.
- **claim-bottom-ledger-surplus** (bound, [source](#stmt-match-bottom-surplus)): At each settled state and every nonempty $X\subseteq\mathcal D$, $$Lh(X)\ge y^a(X)\ge w(X)>0. \label{match:bottom-certificate}$$ Thus $h(X)\ge1$, every bin has weight at most $L$, and a tight set $h(X)=1$ has total weight at most $L$.
  - Explanation: At settlement, the debt-free bottom ledger gives strict Hall surplus on every nonempty bin set. It also bounds individual bin weights and the weight of a tight set.
- **claim-deletion-net-debt** (construction, [source](#stmt-match-deletion)): In a preserved ledger of floor $cw$, redirect each allocation of a surviving row into $R$ to its current assigned edge, adding an equal debt at the recipient bin. This preserves the exterior ledger invariant. If $O$ is redirected mass, $I$ is removed rows’ allocation to the exterior, and $f$ is the number of free bins removed, then $$\begin{split} O&=I+Lf-y(R),\\ I&\le L(h(R)-f),\\ \Delta\!\left(\sum_d p_d\right)=O-p(R)&\le Lh(R)-cw(R). \end{split} \label{match:deletion-debt}$$ The last bound is at most $2L$ at a gain. A gain decreases the represented unmatched count by two; contractions, expansions, and peels leave it unchanged.
  - Explanation: Redirecting surviving row allocations to their assigned edges, with equal compensating debt, preserves the exterior ledger. The net-debt inequality pays for later peels and separates gains from representation changes.
- **claim-local-top-barrier-maintenance** (construction, [source](#stmt-match-top-maintenance)): The top ledger can be maintained on the $\varepsilon$-grid with logarithmic arguments above $\delta$ before pricing and above $\delta/10$ at refreshes. Apart from structural work, repairs cost constantly many explicit point operations per dirty coordinate. Between structural operations a small-allocation repair decreases $\Phi$ by at most $O(\delta)$, and slack repairs never decrease it. Positivity repairs add less than $S^{-800}$ debt on every guarded phase prefix. Moreover, $$\begin{split} \widehat s_d\ge4C&\ \Longrightarrow\ s_d\ge3C,\\ \text{no eligible expansion}&\ \Longrightarrow\ s_d\le5C\quad\text{for every bin with a \(U\)-contact}. \end{split} \label{match:contact-slacks}$$
  - Explanation: Dirty-coordinate repairs preserve positive grid arguments with bounded objective loss and tiny added debt. Cached slack thresholds safely determine expansion eligibility without a full coordinate scan.
- **claim-exact-exchange-projection** (construction, [source](#stmt-match-exchange-projection)): An integral maximum flow in the exchange network produces either a zero-debt ledger of floor $cw$ on the whole state, or a nonempty bin set $R$ whose peel leaves such a ledger on the remaining state. In the latter case, $$Lh(R)<c_jw(R). \label{match:failed-floor}$$ Every preserved coarser ledger loses at least $w(R)$ in net debt, and the number $f$ of new blockers satisfies $$f\le h(R)<\frac{Cw(R)}{L}. \label{match:peel-blockers}$$
  - Explanation: An exact exchange maximum flow either fills the requested residual floor or identifies a peel whose removal leaves a debt-free ledger. The gap between adjacent floors makes every preserved coarser ledger pay for the removed weight.
- **claim-tight-factor-critical-alternative** (construction, [source](#stmt-match-tight-contraction)): Suppose <a href="#match:bottom-certificate" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="match:bottom-certificate">[match:bottom-certificate]</a> holds and an edge joins distinct bins $i,j$. Either the injection can be rearranged to leave both free, or a unique inclusion-minimal $X\supseteq\{i,j\}$ satisfies $h(X)=1$. In the latter case $w(X)\le L$, and the physical block $\bigl(\bigcup_{d\in X}d\bigr)\cup A(X)$ is constructively factor-critical using the joining edge, its row–bin incidences, and its child-bin witnesses.
  - Explanation: A distinct-bin contact permits either simultaneous freeing or contraction of the unique minimal tight set. Hall assignments and child witnesses explicitly realize every requested exposure in the contracted odd block.
- **claim-bounded-randomized-pair-action** (algorithm, [source](#stmt-match-pair-action)): At a settled state, the preceding searches process a queried distinct-bin edge as follows. A returned contraction is the minimal tight set with a valid factor-critical witness; a returned gain has a valid joint-freeing witness. Conditional on the valid current state, the probability of a premature negative answer, including a late-witness abort, is at most $2^{-\sigma}S^{-60}$. Continued contractions cost $O(\|X\|\mathop{\mathrm{polylog}}S)$; a gain attempt or final abort costs $O(L\mathop{\mathrm{polylog}}S)$, with fixed-parameter reliability factors. These work bounds hold on every path.
  - Explanation: Bounded tight-set searches and reverse walks return only valid contractions or gains. Capped unsuccessful searches may return a premature negative answer, with a conditional error allowance and every-path work bounds.
- **claim-bin-materialization** (algorithm, [source](#stmt-match-materialization)): A current bin can be materialized with any prescribed physical exposure in time linear in its physical size up to logarithmic factors. All current bins can be dissolved at an erasure or phase boundary within the corresponding total physical-size bound.
  - Explanation: Stored contraction witnesses can be expanded recursively for a prescribed exposure. Local row augmentations and single visits to children make the total work proportional to the dissolved physical structure, up to logarithmic overhead.
- **claim-local-exact-ledger-projection** (algorithm, [source](#stmt-lem-projection)): Suppose an integral parent ledger satisfies <a href="#proj:parent" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="proj:parent">[proj:parent]</a> in a partition state of a subcubic graph on at most $S$ physical vertices. Its allocations, column residuals, weights, and positive debts support point access and sparse indexed updates. Assume $P=O_a(S)$ for the fixed-parameter asymptotic bound. Then the exact exchange projection can be implemented in $$\label{proj:time} (P+B)S^{O(1/a)+o_a(1)}$$ total time on every path, at all sufficiently large sizes for fixed sufficiently large parameters.* *The implementation either returns the exact exchange-flow changes and, if needed, the full residual reachable set $R_0$; reports a detected stochastic failure; or suspends on a deterministic prerequisite check. It uses $O(\log S)$ priced-step instances. Each reached instance with valid prerequisites has its conditional detected-failure allowance $2^{-\sigma}S^{-60}$. All updates to a fresh child ledger and all necessary physical scans are confined to $O(P)$ represented incidences and bin weight, apart from the padding and subpolynomial factors in <a href="#proj:time" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="proj:time">[proj:time]</a>. The stated deterministic prerequisites do not cause suspension in the sufficiently large fixed-parameter range.
  - Explanation: Sparse parent access and indexed debt updates permit an exact projection confined to debt-sized explored regions. The routine returns flow changes and the full required residual set, or explicitly reports stochastic failure or deterministic suspension.
- **claim-local-threshold-certificate** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-proj-threshold)): An unfailed, nonsuspended threshold test cannot report $\mathrm{LOW}$ if the unlocked optimistic maximum is at least $T$ in scaled units. Its success exit implies that the explored true unscaled maximum is at least $b$.
  - Explanation: In an unfailed nonsuspended test, optimistic capacity above the threshold rules out LOW. A successful exit certifies the corresponding threshold in the explored graph with true capacities.
- **claim-full-residual-cut-recovery** (algorithm, [source](#stmt-proj-local-cut)): Suppose $\beta>0$, and let $v$ be the full unscaled exchange maximum. Suppose a true explored region of maximum $v$ is available when $v>0$, and an unlocked optimistic region of maximum $v$ is available when $v<\beta$. The union of the available regions, using true capacities and original arc identifiers, suffices to compute an exact full exchange flow and, when $v<\beta$, the full residual source-reachable bin set.
  - Explanation: The available success and failure regions recover an exact full-network flow and, when a deficit remains, the entire residual source-reachable bin set. Original arc identities and true capacities are essential to the union.
- **claim-phase-debt-and-structure-accounting** (bound, [source](#stmt-phase-accounting)): In a phase with initial deficit $K$, threshold $L$, and target $g_*$, every guarded prefix before completion satisfies $$\label{phase:peel-budget} \sum_{\rm peels}w(R_0)\le2L(g_*-1)+S^{-800}, \qquad \#\{\text{blockers}\}\le2C(g_*-1).$$ There are $O(N+Lg_*)$ expansions, the sum of contraction arities $\|X\|$ is $O(N+Lg_*)$, and the total tick increase is $\tau=O_a(N)$.
  - Explanation: Repaired gains supply the debt consumed by peels. This bounds erased bin weight, blockers, expansions, contraction arities, and ticks throughout guarded prefixes without a perfect-matching promise.
- **claim-perfect-matching-prevents-stall** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-phase-stall)): If the subcubic graph has a perfect matching, a settled phase state with fewer than $g_*$ gains admits neither a valid $\mathrm{LOW}$ exit nor a permanent lack of progress. More precisely, when neither a distinct-bin contact nor an eligible expansion is available, the top objective has a circulation direction $d$ with $$g^{\mathsf T}d >\tfrac12\\|\lambda d\\|_1 .$$
  - Explanation: A perfect matching supplies an existential family of augmenting paths. At a structural stall, these paths yield a feasible comparison allocation whose circulation direction has sufficient logarithmic ascent to exclude a valid LOW exit.
- **claim-eventual-pm-guard-adequacy** (bound, [source](#stmt-phase-guard-adequacy)): For every sufficiently large fixed $a$ and fixed admissible reliability parameter $\sigma$, all deterministic guards of the PM construction are adequate for all sufficiently large $S$, on every computation path. The bounds just proved apply also to a prefix ending in a failed or interrupted attempted operation. No perfect-matching promise is needed for these time and adequacy assertions.
  - Explanation: Guarded-prefix estimates exclude a first deterministic interruption at sufficiently large fixed parameters and sizes. The argument includes failed attempted operations and does not rely on perfect matchability.
- **claim-fixed-parameter-subcubic-search** (algorithm, [source](#stmt-thm-subcubic)): For the integers $a\ge1000$ and fixed reliability parameters $0\le\sigma\le a+1$, the construction defines a uniform randomized algorithm on loopless subcubic graphs with identified edge copies and $N\le S$ vertices. Every computation halts with a checked perfect matching, a negative answer, or a suspension. It has no false positive. For each fixed input with a perfect matching, the probability of a negative answer is at most $$\label{phase:pm-error} 2^{-\sigma}S^{-5}.$$ This statement bounds erroneous answers, not suspensions, and holds conditionally for adaptive invocations with fresh randomness and valid prerequisites.* *For every sufficiently large fixed $a$ and fixed admissible $\sigma$, there is no suspension on any path at all sufficiently large $S$. The total time on every path is then $$\label{phase:pm-time} S^{1+O(1/a)+o_a(1)},$$ where the exponent coefficients in $O(1/a)$ are absolute. All initialization, physical data movement, arithmetic, random bits, and explicit witness writing are included.
  - Explanation: The fixed-parameter subcubic procedure has checked positive witnesses and bounded erroneous negative answers. Suspensions are a separate outcome; sufficiently large fixed parameters eventually eliminate them on every path.
- **claim-priced-logarithmic-step-interface** (algorithm, [source](#stmt-lem-priced-steps)): Suppose a nominal bound $R$ counts the logical vertex and edge births and endpoint insertions over one instance, including retargetings. Mere value or price changes on retained topology need not be counted as new incidences. Assume $$\label{engine:nominal-size} R+B\le S^{1+2/a}.$$ There is a uniform implementation with the following properties.* 1. *A pricing call may return <span class="nodecor"><span class="smallcaps">Low</span></span>, but it cannot do so if there is a nonzero circulation $d$ satisfying $$\label{engine:high-direction} g^{\mathsf T}d\ge\tfrac12\\|\lambda d\\|_1,$$ unless it reports a detected failure or suspends.* 2. *A call not returning <span class="nodecor"><span class="smallcaps">Low</span></span>, failure, or suspension performs an exact increment $h\in\varepsilon\mathbb Z^E$ that is a circulation and satisfies $$\label{engine:step-guarantee} \\|\lambda h\\|_1\le q/100, \qquad g^{\mathsf T}h\ge q^2/1000.$$ It leaves all logarithmic arguments positive and increases $\Phi$ by $\Omega(q^2)$.* 3. *The implementation exposes registers $\widehat f_e$. After settling updates and before the next pricing call, the descriptors are valid at these registers and $$\label{engine:register-guarantee} \lambda_e(\widehat f_e)\|f_e-\widehat f_e\|\le q/100.$$ An explicit client setting can reanchor the coordinate exactly. Apart from such client settings, the number of refresh notifications is at most the number of successful pricing steps times $S^{O(1/a)+o_a(1)}$. Notifications are supplied as a list of dirty coordinates. Maintaining the representation without taking a step causes no implicit primal motion.* 4. *With $Q$ external pricing, point, and edge operations, total time, excluding the client’s own work, is $$\label{engine:time} (R+B+Q)S^{O(1/a)+o_a(1)}.$$ The bound is on every computation path and includes initialization, arithmetic, random bits, and all representation changes. Polynomially many operations are allowed, with a loose guard $Q\le S^{100}$.* *The conditional probability of detected failure in the entire instance is at most $2^{-\sigma}S^{-60}$, including when the instance is chosen adaptively. Deterministic size, precision, and representation checks may suspend, but none does so for all sufficiently large $S$ at each sufficiently large fixed $a$ and each fixed $\sigma\ge0$ within the stated hypotheses.
  - Explanation: The priced-step implementation provides exact grid circulation increments, logarithmic progress, valid cached registers, and sparse notifications. Its total cost and conditional instance failure budget include adaptive clients and representation changes.
- **claim-dynamic-flat-cycle-interface** (algorithm, [source](#stmt-lem-flat-cycle)): Let $M=2^u$, where $u$ is divisible by $2a^2$, and suppose $$\label{engine:backend-size-range} S^{1/a}\le M\le S^2.$$ Consider a dynamic undirected graph with reference orientations and fixed vertex slots, of total current size at most $M$ and maximum degree at most three. It has positive integral lengths $\ell_e$ and integral signed gradients $\gamma_e$, both of magnitude at most $M^{1200a}$. Insertions and deletions are arbitrary; a price replacement is a deletion followed by an insertion. Put $$\mathop{\mathrm{OPT}}=\max_{d\ne0,\ d\text{ a circulation}} \frac{\|\gamma^{\mathsf T}d\|}{\\|\ell d\\|_1},$$ with value zero if there is no nonzero circulation.* *The backend exposes a forest. Every forest vertex maps to an input vertex slot; every forest edge either maps to a current input edge with specified endpoint correspondence, or is null and joins equal images. The multiplicity over each input slot is at most $M^{10/a}/4$. A query returns a list of oriented single input edges and oriented forest paths, the latter specified by endpoints. Each piece is used with unit multiplicity, with repeated pieces listed separately. Their signed physical projection is a circulation $c$. Its *raw length* $V$ is the sum of input lengths over all mapped edge traversals with repetition; a null forest traversal contributes zero. If $\mathop{\mathrm{OPT}}>0$, the answer has $V>0$ and $$\label{engine:flat-quality} \frac{\|\gamma^{\mathsf T}c\|}{V} \ge M^{-10/a}\mathop{\mathrm{OPT}}.$$ An empty or missing answer is permitted when $\mathop{\mathrm{OPT}}=0$. There are $M^{O(1/a)+o_a(1)}$ pieces in an answer.* *Initialization costs $M^{1+O(1/a)+o_a(1)}$. Amortized edit and query costs, including explicitly exposed forest logs, are $M^{O(1/a)+o_a(1)}$, with bounds on every path. Retained forest occurrences and edges have stable lifetimes. Endpoint or mapping changes are replacements, and removals are exposed before the old mappings or input data are invalidated. Transactions permit deletion-first old-to-final updates with no intervening path query.* *The bounds apply over the polynomial lifespans required by Lemma <a href="#lem:priced-steps" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:priced-steps">21</a>, and against adaptive clients. Reliability can be chosen so that detected failure in an entire engine instance has conditional probability at most $2^{-\sigma}S^{-60}$. A flagged state has no quality requirement. Deterministic prerequisite checks may suspend, with eventual adequacy as in Lemma <a href="#lem:priced-steps" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:priced-steps">21</a>.
  - Explanation: The backend maintains a mapped forest and returns a circulation represented by individual edges and forest paths. Its quality denominator counts raw traversals before cancellation, and stable lifetimes support exact client accounting.
- **claim-augmented-dynamic-tree-cost** (bound, [source](#stmt-engine-tree-cost)): On a forest of at most $N_f$ subdivided nodes, the preceding operations use $O(\log N_f)$ amortized tree primitives per link, cut, evert, expose, point operation, or lazy path update. Reporting and disabling a witnessed threshold violation costs the same amortized amount per reported node. Each primitive uses a constant number of operations on the stored fields.
  - Explanation: Signed tags, raw-length aggregates, and witnessed threshold minima add bounded field work to the preferred-path operations. Reporting cost is charged per actual reported node.
- **claim-register-accuracy-under-forest-churn** (bound, [source](#stmt-engine-refresh-bound)): After settlement, <a href="#engine:register-guarantee" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="engine:register-guarantee">[engine:register-guarantee]</a> holds. The total number of engine refresh reports and copy-disable events is $O(D)$ times the number of successful pricing steps, apart from explicit client operations. The assertion is unaffected by arbitrary forest churn, dormant-slot reuse, or client reanchoring.
  - Explanation: Absolute motion is conserved through copy death and dormant reuse, then consumed at reanchoring. This bounds register error and notifications independently of forest churn, apart from charged client operations.
- **claim-static-resource-routing-sparsifier** (algorithm, [source](#stmt-lem-routing-helper)): Let $F$ be an explicitly listed undirected multigraph with identified edge copies, and let $v_+$ be its number of incident vertex labels. For each edge $f$, an explicit list specifies resource counts $m_{xf}\in\{1,\ldots,10\}$; unlisted counts are zero. Suppose that $$\deg_F(v)\le C_0,\qquad \sum_{f\in F}m_{xf}\le C_0 \quad\text{for every vertex $v$ and resource $x$},$$ where $C_0\ge1$ is an integer, degrees count incidences, and both $2\|F\|$ and the total resource-list length are at most $M^2$. Assume that identifiers and numerical input data have $(\log S)^{O_a(1)}$ bits. There is a uniform randomized algorithm that either reports detected failure or returns a set $F'\subseteq F$ and, for every $f=vw\in F$, an explicit walk $Q_f$ from $v$ to $w$ in $F'$, such that $$\label{route:guarantees} \|F'\|\le P_*v_+,\qquad \|Q_f\|\le P_*,\qquad \sum_{f\in F}\ \sum_{e\text{ traversed in }Q_f}m_{xe} \le P_*C_0\quad\text{for every $x$}.$$ The inner sum counts traversal multiplicities and ignores orientation. A loop demand may be assigned the empty walk. Every returned result is verified. For each fixed reliability parameter $\sigma\ge0$, the time on every computation path is $$\label{route:time} (1+\text{input list size})\,b\,M^{o_a(1)},$$ and the probability of detected failure is at most $2^{-\sigma}S^{-800}$. The same statement holds conditionally when the input is determined by earlier computation and this call uses fresh bits.
  - Explanation: Verified representative edges route every demand with bounded support, hops, and total resource load. Repeated traversal is charged regardless of orientation, and rejected randomized attempts obey the same capped work accounting.
- **claim-sampled-cut-and-resource-concentration** (bound, [source](#stmt-route-sampling)): For a sampled piece, except with probability at most $4s'\exp[-K_s/(100\chi^2)]$, every vertex set $X$ satisfies $$\label{route:cut-approximation} \left\|\sum_{e\in\partial X\text{ retained}}\omega_e -\|\partial X\|\right\| \le\frac{\min\{\mathop{\mathrm{vol}}(X),\mathop{\mathrm{vol}}(\bar X)\}}{4\chi}.$$ This event implies the total-weight check in <a href="#route:sample-checks" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="route:sample-checks">[route:sample-checks]</a>; the sample-count check also holds with exponentially high probability in $K_s$. Conditional on the input pieces at a fixed depth, for each resource $x$, $$\label{route:sample-resources} \sum_{\substack{e\text{ retained}\\\text{over all pieces at this depth}}} \omega_e m_{xe}\le2C_0$$ except with probability at most $\exp(-K_s/30)$.
  - Explanation: Degree-normalized sampling simultaneously controls every cut. Conditional resource concentration across disjoint pieces bounds weighted resource totals, while separate checks control sample count and total weight.
- **claim-median-overlay-conductance** (construction, [source](#stmt-route-overlay)): There is a median rule for which a piece completing all $r_m$ rounds has an $r_m$-regular overlay of conductance at least $1/(4r_m)$, except with probability at most $4N_p^3\exp[-r_m/(80\Lambda)]$. This guarantee holds for any choice of perfect matching across each chosen pair of halves.
  - Explanation: Fresh sign projections and median halves force expected contraction of the averaging potential. The resulting regular overlay has a conductance guarantee for arbitrary perfect matchings across the selected halves.
- **claim-lazy-walk-conductance-mixing** (bound, [source](#stmt-route-cheeger)): In a connected undirected multigraph with at least two vertices and conductance $\phi$, the lazy walk has spectral gap at least $\phi^2/4$. If the graph has at most $N_p$ labels, degree ratio at most $D_c\le\Lambda^{1/20}$, and conductance at least $1/(4r_m)$, then for every $h\ge t$, $$\label{route:pointwise-mixing} \tfrac12\pi(y)\le P^h(x,y)\le2\pi(y) \quad\text{for all $x,y$}.$$
  - Explanation: The graph-conductance normalization yields a lazy spectral-gap bound valid for multigraphs. Under the stated degree and conductance regime, this implies uniform pointwise mixing bounds.
- **claim-recursive-packet-routing** (algorithm, [source](#stmt-route-router)): Conditional on an overlay satisfying Lemma <a href="#route:overlay" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="route:overlay">27</a>, the procedure above produces explicit routes for all packets with both maximum length and total traversal congestion on each overlay edge at most $H_*$, except with probability less than $M^{-20}$ throughout the checked parameter range. All its data sizes and running times remain bounded by the specified caps even when it fails.
  - Explanation: Group-conditioned packet and virtual-job walks produce explicit routes with bounded length and edge congestion on a valid overlay. Shrinking groups and capped rejection also bound failed computations.
- **claim-bottom-level-cycle-search** (algorithm, [source](#stmt-back-base)): At level $d$, a query can return individual oriented edges forming a circulation with certified score at least $\mathop{\mathrm{OPT}}_d/2$, whenever $\mathop{\mathrm{OPT}}_d>0$. Initialization and each graph operation or query cost $M^{O(1/a)+o_a(1)}$, and the answer has at most $n_d=M^{2/a}$ pieces.
  - Explanation: Exact dyadic threshold tests detect negative directed cycles in the traversal graph. The best witness gives a certified approximation to the circulation ratio using explicitly listed edges.
- **claim-verified-padded-covers** (construction, [source](#stmt-back-covers)): For every layer $j$ one can construct $K_h$ partitions of the frozen vertices into cells, with a shortest-path tree in each cell, such that:* 1. *every cell tree has radius at most $\Gamma R_j$ about its center;* 2. *for every vertex, at least one of the partitions has a cell containing its entire closed $R_j$-ball in $\bar G$.* *The properties are verified before use. The construction takes $n_iM^{o_a(1)}$ time on every path for fixed reliability parameter $\sigma$, with detected-failure probability at most $2^{-\sigma}S^{-800}$ conditional on any valid frozen input. It uses integer operations and fresh fair random bits.
  - Explanation: Capped geometric shifts construct shortest-path cells. Radius and winning-margin checks certify padding before the covers are used, so later adaptive states need not trust an unverified sampling event.
- **claim-finite-routing-forest-family** (construction, [source](#stmt-back-forest-family)): There is a family of at most $(K_h^2(u+1))^{O(r)}$ forests $T^0$ with the following property. At each layer, any two distinct lower slots can be prescribed arbitrary OUT options, and some family member realizes all these prescriptions simultaneously across the layers. Each member contains separate copies of all cell trees and IN links, and exactly one OUT link per lower slot.
  - Explanation: Bit-selected OUT options produce a finite family of forests. Distinct lower slots can be assigned arbitrary options, simultaneously across layers, while the upward orientation prevents cycles.
- **claim-fixed-marginal-upward-routes** (theorem, [source](#stmt-back-path-law)): The independent layer choices $\mathcal L_j(v)$ define an upward path $P(v)$ in the layered system with all OUT options. For every frozen edge $e=vw$, its two path laws admit a coupling such that the closed walk $e+P(w)-P(v)$, after canceling a common final suffix, has expected full length at most $A_*\ell_e$. The marginal law of $P(v)$ is the same for every incident edge used in this assertion.
  - Explanation: Origin-indexed cell laws admit coupled endpoint routes with bounded expected closing-walk length. Preserving each vertex's marginal across incident edges is what permits later imbalance cancellation.
- **claim-frozen-decomposition-with-holes** (theorem, [source](#stmt-back-frozen-decomposition)): Fix any state of the epoch and any circulation $\Delta$ on its current nonloops. Write $\Delta=\Delta_s+\Delta_n$ for its surviving frozen and new coordinates. There are real coefficients $\alpha_f$ on live fundamental chords and a vector $z$ on the unduplicated raw forests $H_j$ such that, before restricting the frozen coordinates, $$\begin{aligned} \sum_f\alpha_f\pi C_f^0&=\Delta_s+\pi z, \label{back:frozen-identity}\\ \sum_f\|\alpha_f\|w_f&\le A_*\\|\ell\Delta_s\\|_1, \label{back:coefficient-bound}\\ \\|z\\|_{\mathrm{length}}&\le A_*\\|\ell\Delta_s\\|_1. \label{back:raw-residual-bound} \end{aligned}$$ Moreover $z$ is the raw-tree part of $$\label{back:marginal-cancellation} \sum_v\bigl(\operatorname{in}_{\Delta_s}(v) -\operatorname{out}_{\Delta_s}(v)\bigr)\mathbb E[P(v)].$$ Consequently this description uses only upward paths starting at slots incident to new edges. If $z_L$ is the restriction of $z$ to live raw forest edges, then $$\label{back:clipped-identity} \sum_f\alpha_f\pi C_f^{\mathrm{clip}}-\pi z_L =\Delta_s.$$
  - Explanation: Fixed route marginals decompose surviving frozen coordinates into live-chord fundamental vectors and a raw-forest remainder. Restriction after holes preserves the exact projected identity, without making individual clipped vectors circulations.
- **claim-postorder-portal-grid** (construction, [source](#stmt-back-portals)): A forest admits a set of at most its number of vertices divided by $k$ portals such that every component remaining after portal removal has fewer than $k$ vertices. The set is constructed by one postorder traversal.
  - Explanation: A postorder count marks portals only after accumulating enough unmarked vertices. Disjoint charges bound the portal count and leave every remaining pocket below the prescribed size.
- **claim-exact-terminal-forest-compression** (construction, [source](#stmt-back-compression)): If a vector on a forest has divergence only at a specified terminal set, it vanishes off the terminal Steiner subtrees and has constant oriented coefficient along each suppressed segment. Compression therefore preserves its gradient and its length norm exactly.
  - Explanation: A forest vector supported by terminal divergences is zero outside terminal Steiner subtrees. Degree-two suppression then preserves its gradient and length norm exactly.
- **claim-one-level-cycle-quality-reduction** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-backend-reduction)): In every state of an epoch, suppose the active logical path assignments use selected chords and have at most $p_*$ hops. Let $L_{\mathrm{best}}$ be the largest local correction score <a href="#back:local-score" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="back:local-score">[back:local-score]</a>, or zero if there is none. Then, for every nonzero circulation $\Delta$ on the current nonloops, $$\label{back:reduction-bound} \frac{\|\gamma^{\mathsf T}\Delta\|}{\\|\ell\Delta\\|_1} \le 2Z_*A_*L_{\mathrm{best}}+12Z_*A_*\mathop{\mathrm{OPT}}(J) \le b^2\max\{L_{\mathrm{best}},\mathop{\mathrm{OPT}}(J)\}.$$ This conclusion is simultaneous for all current circulations and requires no independence between the state and the verified covers or path assignments.
  - Explanation: Every parent circulation is compared with local correction candidates and a balanced circulation in the joint child graph. Controlled coefficient-weighted lengths yield a simultaneous quality bound for adaptive states.
- **claim-recursive-certified-cycle-quality** (bound, [source](#stmt-back-quality-induction)): Assume the path assignments and public forest lifting of Section <a href="#sec:backend-updates" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:backend-updates">8</a>. The backend at level $i$ returns a certified score at least $$\label{back:level-score} \frac12 b^{-2(d-i)}\mathop{\mathrm{OPT}}_i.$$ At the outer level this is at least $M^{-10/a}\mathop{\mathrm{OPT}}$.
  - Explanation: The bottom certified score propagates through the quality reduction. Public lifting preserves gradient and does not increase raw length, so recursive scores remain valid at the parent.
- **claim-local-logical-class-edits** (construction, [source](#stmt-update-classes)): The leg assignments of Section <a href="#sec:backend-quality" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:backend-quality">7</a> can be maintained with logical degree at most $D_\ell$. One input edit induces only a logarithmic factor many logical edit units across all tree–width graphs, where a unit is a class split, a pair of fragment-class births at a cluster activation, or a single chord death. Each unit involves at most $O(D_\ell)$ incidence work. Every change to the leg of a surviving active incidence entails a split of its class.
  - Explanation: Holes and chord deaths induce bounded logical units across tree–width graphs. A surviving incidence can change its leg only through a class split, which supplies a precise trigger for snapshot invalidation.
- **claim-anchor-core-snapshot-rebuild** (algorithm, [source](#stmt-update-anchor-core)): Suppose the interval from a parent snapshot to a rebuild contains $E_0$ logical edit units in one tree–width graph. The rebuild can restore <a href="#update:snapshot-bounds" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="update:snapshot-bounds">[update:snapshot-bounds]</a> using a helper instance on $O(E_0)$ incident labels. It selects at most $O(P_*E_0)$ additional central chords. The work is $$O(1+D_\ell E_0)\,bM^{o_a(1)}, \label{update:rebuild-work}$$ in addition to scanning the interval’s edit log with logarithmic overhead per tick and explicitly handled incidence.
  - Explanation: Only paths visiting marked ancestors require replacement. Their unchanged prefixes and suffixes connect to a small anchor core, whose resource-preserving routes restore the snapshot bounds with limited new support.
- **claim-cumulative-support-and-assignment-work** (bound, [source](#stmt-update-support)): The initial assignments use at most $h_i$ times a logarithmic factor many support chords. On every epoch prefix of $t'\le h_i$ edits, the additional support selections number at most $bt'$ times a logarithmic factor, and local assignment work is $t'M^{O(1/a)+o_a(1)}$ after initialization. Initialization costs $n_iM^{O(1/a)+o_a(1)}$.
  - Explanation: Hierarchical rebuild periods bound total central-chord selections over an epoch prefix. Previously selected live chords remain stored, while local assignment maintenance has the claimed amortized cost.
- **claim-small-compressed-child-edits** (construction, [source](#stmt-update-compressed-edits)): The single child graph $J$ can be initialized nonempty. Throughout an epoch it uses at most $h_ib$ times a logarithmic factor many active slots and edges, including slots assigned earlier in that epoch. On an epoch prefix of $t'$ further input edits it receives at most $bt'$ times a logarithmic factor individual edits. Its degree is a logarithmic factor, and its integral prices obey the level-$(i+1)$ bounds. The local construction work per parent edit is $M^{O(1/a)+o_a(1)}$ amortized.
  - Explanation: Persistent terminal promotions and deletion-first segment replacements maintain a single small child graph. The child receives fewer edits than the parent's local maintenance work and retains bounded degree and integral prices.
- **claim-public-flat-forest-lifting** (construction, [source](#stmt-update-flat-lift)): The construction is a forest with valid current actual or null edge maps. A child forest-path piece lifts to at most one public forest-path piece, preserving its signed projection and with parent raw length at most its child raw length. An individual child-edge piece lifts to one path or one actual-edge piece, or can be omitted if null. The lifted sum of a child circulation is a circulation with the same gradient. The number of returned pieces is $M^{O(1/a)+o_a(1)}$.
  - Explanation: Prebuilt arms and null connectors lift child paths by endpoint lookup. Forest cancellation preserves signed projection and can only decrease raw length; no long lifted path must be expanded to return its representation.
- **claim-public-occurrence-multiplicity** (bound, [source](#stmt-update-multiplicity)): For all sufficiently large $M$ at fixed parameters, level $i$ has at most $D_i$ public forest vertices over each input slot. In particular the outer multiplicity is at most $M^{10/a}/4$.
  - Explanation: Disjoint segment interiors prevent segment length from multiplying occurrence counts. The gap between successive pool bounds absorbs the fixed-parameter logarithmic overhead.
- **claim-persistent-public-forest-recourse** (construction, [source](#stmt-update-recourse)): For one parent input edit within an epoch, let $Q$ be the set of child labels assigned or changed, or incident to a changed child edge or changed segment realization. Apart from local full-copy edits, the public forest can be updated in $$\|Q\|D_{i+1}(k+1)\log^{O_a(1)}(2+M) +\mathcal L\log^{O_a(1)}(2+M) \label{update:recourse-cost}$$ work and public log records, where $\mathcal L$ is the number of child log records processed. Retained arm edges at labels outside $Q$ keep their lifetimes and maps, even if their arm slot is reassigned to a different child occurrence.
  - Explanation: Affected child labels pay for arm reconstruction; general child log records pay only their own processing overhead. Dormant arms at unaffected labels retain identities and maps even when assigned to new occurrences.
- **claim-recursive-work-and-log-bounds** (bound, [source](#stmt-update-recurrences)): For a prefix of $s$ graph operations, counting queries and edits and including an operation that terminates partway through, level $i$ has total work and explicit public-log size at most $n_iA_i+sU_i$, where $$A_i,U_i=M^{O(1/a)+o_a(1)}.$$ All coefficients in the $O(1/a)$ exponents are absolute.
  - Explanation: The recursive accounting separates local reconstruction from child edits and public log processing. Small child edit multipliers preserve an absolute coefficient in the near-linear exponent, including partial failed operations.
- **claim-backend-guards-and-adaptive-reliability** (bound, [source](#stmt-update-reliability)): The construction can enforce all size, multiplicity, arithmetic, and sampling prerequisites by finite checks. For every sufficiently large fixed $a$ and every fixed $\sigma\ge0$, those deterministic prerequisites are adequate for all sufficiently large sizes in the permitted engine range, on every random path. Conditional on any adaptively specified history before an invocation, the detected sampling failure probability over an entire engine instance is at most $2^{-\sigma}S^{-60}$.
  - Explanation: Finite checks certify every published representation and enforce unconditional caps on attempted constructions. Fresh conditional sampling budgets sum over the engine lifetime, while inadequate deterministic checks suspend rather than issue sampling failure.
- **claim-uniform-permutation-network** (construction, [source](#stmt-uniform-network)): For every power of two $s\ge1$, there is a directed acyclic network with $s$ distinct input positions and $s$ distinct output positions, of size $O(s\log(s+2))$, in which every permutation of inputs to outputs is routable by vertex-disjoint directed paths. A position may be both an input and an output when $s=1$. The network can be constructed uniformly within its size bound.
  - Explanation: Recursive upper and lower switching networks route any permutation by disjoint paths. Alternating colors on the input-pair/output-pair cycles select the recursive routes.
- **claim-cardinality-to-perfect-matching** (construction, [source](#stmt-uniform-cardinality)): Given $G$ and an integer $0\le k\le\lfloor n/2\rfloor$, one can construct in $O(m+n\log(n+2))$ time a loopless graph $H_k$ of that size such that $H_k$ has a perfect matching if and only if $G$ has a matching of size $k$. A perfect matching of $H_k$ yields an explicit size-$k$ matching of $G$ in linear time in the constructed size.
  - Explanation: Split permutation-network positions and mandatory sinks encode precisely the uncovered original vertices. Selecting original edges from a perfect matching decodes the requested matching cardinality.
- **claim-witness-preserving-subcubic-reduction** (construction, [source](#stmt-uniform-degree)): A loopless graph with $v$ vertices and $e$ edges either has an isolated vertex, certifying absence of a perfect matching, or can be replaced in $O(v+e)$ time by an equivalent perfect-matching instance of maximum degree three and size $O(v+e)$. Witnesses can be converted in either direction within the same bound.
  - Explanation: Each nonisolated vertex is replaced by an odd path with old incidences attached at odd positions. A perfect matching uses exactly one external incidence per gadget, and witnesses convert in both directions.
- **claim-fixed-branch-every-path-performance** (bound, [source](#stmt-uniform-fixed-branch)): For every sufficiently large fixed $t$, branch $t$ avoids deterministic suspension on all sufficiently large inputs, on every computation path. Its total instruction count, including unsuccessful tests, threshold search, and explicit witness handling, is $$p^{1+O(1/a)+o_a(1)}.$$ The coefficient in $O(1/a)$ is absolute.
  - Explanation: For a sufficiently large fixed branch index, all deterministic prerequisites eventually hold on every random path. Reductions, unsuccessful threshold tests, and witness handling share the fixed-parameter instruction bound.
- **claim-single-physical-word-program** (construction, [source](#stmt-uniform-physical-ram)): The schedule is realizable by one finite program with a fixed physical word constant $c_0$. On every path, including failed paths, it halts after polynomially many instructions and uses polynomially many addressed words, with constants independent of the input.
  - Explanation: Instruction-level scheduling gives the deterministic fallback enough quota to halt. Charging every allocation first bounds all physical storage polynomially, allowing a single fixed physical word constant.
- **claim-uniform-prescribed-degree-factor-search** (algorithm, [source](#stmt-cor-f-factor)): There are a single uniform randomized word-RAM algorithm, fixed constants $c_f\ge2$ and $C_f\ge1$, and a nonincreasing function $\eta_f:\mathbb N_{\ge1}\to\mathbb R_{\ge0}$ with $\eta_f(q)\to0$ as $q\to\infty$ such that the following holds. The input is a simple graph $G=([n],E)$ with $n\ge1$, $m=\|E\|$, and $p=n+m$, in the adjacency-list representation of Section <a href="#sec:introduction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:introduction">1</a>, together with one-word integers $f(v)$ satisfying $0\le f(v)\le\deg_G(v)$ for all $v$. With the instruction set and costs of Section <a href="#sec:introduction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:introduction">1</a> and word length $w_f=\lceil c_f\log_2(n+2)\rceil$, the algorithm halts on every computation path in at most $$C_f p^{1+\eta_f(p)}$$ instructions. Every output is either an explicit edge list of a valid $f$-factor or <span class="nodecor">NO</span>. If an $f$-factor exists, it returns one with probability at least $2/3$; otherwise it always returns <span class="nodecor">NO</span>.
  - Explanation: A single randomized program decides and constructs prescribed-degree edge subsets in almost-linear input time. Every positive output is checked; failed matching calls can cause false negatives, but an infeasible factor instance always returns NO.
- **claim-linearithmic-factor-to-perfect-matching** (construction, [source](#stmt-factor-reduction)): For the input of Corollary <a href="#cor:f-factor" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:f-factor">53</a>, one can construct a possibly empty simple graph $H_f$ with $\|V(H_f)\|+\|E(H_f)\|=O(m\log(n+2))$ such that $H_f$ has a perfect matching if and only if $G$ has an $f$-factor. The construction takes $O(n+m\log(n+2))$ word instructions and records. A perfect matching of $H_f$ yields an explicit $f$-factor within the same bound.
  - Explanation: Distinct incidence ports and a separate local permutation network enforce the prescribed degree at each host vertex. Selected cross-edge labels decode a factor from a perfect matching of the auxiliary simple graph.

        "anchor": "stmt-match-top-maintenance",
        "exact_quote": "The top ledger can be maintained on the $\\varepsilon$-grid with logarithmic arguments above $\\delta$ before pricing and above $\\delta/10$ at refreshes. Apart from structural work, repairs cost constantly many explicit point operations per dirty coordinate. Between structural operations a small-allocation repair decreases $\\Phi$ by at most $O(\\delta)$, and slack repairs never decrease it. Positivity repairs add less than $S^{-800}$ debt on every guarded phase prefix. Moreover, $$\\begin{split}\n \\widehat s_d\\ge4C&\\ \\Longrightarrow\\ s_d\\ge3C,\\\\\n \\text{no eligible expansion}&\\ \\Longrightarrow\\\n s_d\\le5C\\quad\\text{for every bin with a \\(U\\)-contact}.\n \\end{split}\n \\label{match:contact-slacks}$$",

        "exact_quote": "Suppose an integral parent ledger satisfies <a href=\"#proj:parent\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"proj:parent\">[proj:parent]</a> in a partition state of a subcubic graph on at most $S$ physical vertices. Its allocations, column residuals, weights, and positive debts support point access and sparse indexed updates. Assume $P=O_a(S)$ for the fixed-parameter asymptotic bound. Then the exact exchange projection can be implemented in $$\\label{proj:time}\n                         (P+B)S^{O(1/a)+o_a(1)}$$ total time on every path, at all sufficiently large sizes for fixed sufficiently large parameters.*\n\n*The implementation either returns the exact exchange-flow changes and, if needed, the full residual reachable set $R_0$; reports a detected stochastic failure; or suspends on a deterministic prerequisite check. It uses $O(\\log S)$ priced-step instances. Each reached instance with valid prerequisites has its conditional detected-failure allowance $2^{-\\sigma}S^{-60}$. All updates to a fresh child ledger and all necessary physical scans are confined to $O(P)$ represented incidences and bin weight, apart from the padding and subpolynomial factors in <a href=\"#proj:time\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"proj:time\">[proj:time]</a>.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-subcubic",
        "exact_quote": "For the integers $a\\ge1000$ and fixed reliability parameters $0\\le\\sigma\\le a+1$, the construction defines a uniform randomized algorithm on loopless subcubic graphs with identified edge copies and $N\\le S$ vertices. Every computation halts with a checked perfect matching, a negative answer, or a suspension. It has no false positive. For each fixed input with a perfect matching, the probability of a negative answer is at most $$\\label{phase:pm-error}\n                            2^{-\\sigma}S^{-5}.$$ This statement bounds erroneous answers, not suspensions, and holds conditionally for adaptive invocations with fresh randomness and valid prerequisites.*\n\n\n*For every sufficiently large fixed $a$ and fixed admissible $\\sigma$, there is no suspension on any path at all sufficiently large $S$. The total time on every path is then $$\\label{phase:pm-time}\n                              S^{1+O(1/a)+o_a(1)},$$ where the exponent coefficients in $O(1/a)$ are absolute. All initialization, physical data movement, arithmetic, random bits, and explicit witness writing are included.",
        "source_id": "PM-120.1"
      },

        {

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-priced-steps",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose a nominal bound $R$ counts the logical vertex and edge births and endpoint insertions over one instance, including retargetings. Mere value or price changes on retained topology need not be counted as new incidences. Assume $$\\label{engine:nominal-size}\n                         R+B\\le S^{1+2/a}.$$ There is a uniform implementation with the following properties.*\n\n\n1.  *A pricing call may return <span class=\"nodecor\"><span class=\"smallcaps\">Low</span></span>, but it cannot do so if there is a nonzero circulation $d$ satisfying $$\\label{engine:high-direction}\n                        g^{\\mathsf T}d\\ge\\tfrac12\\|\\lambda d\\|_1,$$ unless it reports a detected failure or suspends.*\n\n\n2.  *A call not returning <span class=\"nodecor\"><span class=\"smallcaps\">Low</span></span>, failure, or suspension performs an exact increment $h\\in\\varepsilon\\mathbb Z^E$ that is a circulation and satisfies $$\\label{engine:step-guarantee}\n           \\|\\lambda h\\|_1\\le q/100,\n           \\qquad g^{\\mathsf T}h\\ge q^2/1000.$$ It leaves all logarithmic arguments positive and increases $\\Phi$ by $\\Omega(q^2)$.*\n\n\n3.  *The implementation exposes registers $\\widehat f_e$. After settling updates and before the next pricing call, the descriptors are valid at these registers and $$\\label{engine:register-guarantee}\n           \\lambda_e(\\widehat f_e)|f_e-\\widehat f_e|\\le q/100.$$ An explicit client setting can reanchor the coordinate exactly. Apart from such client settings, the number of refresh notifications is at most the number of successful pricing steps times $S^{O(1/a)+o_a(1)}$. Notifications are supplied as a list of dirty coordinates. Maintaining the representation without taking a step causes no implicit primal motion.*\n\n\n4.  *With $Q$ external pricing, point, and edge operations, total time, excluding the client’s own work, is $$\\label{engine:time}\n                         (R+B+Q)S^{O(1/a)+o_a(1)}.$$ The bound is on every computation path and includes initialization, arithmetic, random bits, and all representation changes. Polynomially many operations are allowed, with a loose guard $Q\\le S^{100}$.*\n\n*The conditional probability of detected failure in the entire instance is at most $2^{-\\sigma}S^{-60}$, including when the instance is chosen adaptively. Deterministic size, precision, and representation checks may suspend, but none does so for all sufficiently large $S$ at each sufficiently large fixed $a$ and each fixed $\\sigma\\ge0$ within the stated hypotheses.",
        "source_id": "PM-120.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-priced-steps",
          "exact_quote": "Suppose a nominal bound $R$ counts the logical vertex and edge births and endpoint insertions over one instance, including retargetings. Mere value or price changes on retained topology need not be counted as new incidences. Assume $$\\label{engine:nominal-size}\n                         R+B\\le S^{1+2/a}.$$ There is a uniform implementation with the following properties.*\n\n\n1.  *A pricing call may return <span class=\"nodecor\"><span class=\"smallcaps\">Low</span></span>, but it cannot do so if there is a nonzero circulation $d$ satisfying $$\\label{engine:high-direction}\n                        g^{\\mathsf T}d\\ge\\tfrac12\\|\\lambda d\\|_1,$$ unless it reports a detected failure or suspends.*\n\n\n2.  *A call not returning <span class=\"nodecor\"><span class=\"smallcaps\">Low</span></span>, failure, or suspension performs an exact increment $h\\in\\varepsilon\\mathbb Z^E$ that is a circulation and satisfies $$\\label{engine:step-guarantee}\n           \\|\\lambda h\\|_1\\le q/100,\n           \\qquad g^{\\mathsf T}h\\ge q^2/1000.$$ It leaves all logarithmic arguments positive and increases $\\Phi$ by $\\Omega(q^2)$.*\n\n\n3.  *The implementation exposes registers $\\widehat f_e$. After settling updates and before the next pricing call, the descriptors are valid at these registers and $$\\label{engine:register-guarantee}\n           \\lambda_e(\\widehat f_e)|f_e-\\widehat f_e|\\le q/100.$$ An explicit client setting can reanchor the coordinate exactly. Apart from such client settings, the number of refresh notifications is at most the number of successful pricing steps times $S^{O(1/a)+o_a(1)}$. Notifications are supplied as a list of dirty coordinates. Maintaining the representation without taking a step causes no implicit primal motion.*\n\n\n4.  *With $Q$ external pricing, point, and edge operations, total time, excluding the client’s own work, is $$\\label{engine:time}\n                         (R+B+Q)S^{O(1/a)+o_a(1)}.$$ The bound is on every computation path and includes initialization, arithmetic, random bits, and all representation changes. Polynomially many operations are allowed, with a loose guard $Q\\le S^{100}$.*\n\n*The conditional probability of detected failure in the entire instance is at most $2^{-\\sigma}S^{-60}$, including when the instance is chosen adaptively. Deterministic size, precision, and representation checks may suspend, but none does so for all sufficiently large $S$ at each sufficiently large fixed $a$ and each fixed $\\sigma\\ge0$ within the stated hypotheses.",

      "exact_statement": "Suppose a nominal bound $R$ counts the logical vertex and edge births and endpoint insertions over one instance, including retargetings. Assume $$\\label{engine:nominal-size}\n                         R+B\\le S^{1+2/a}.$$ There is a uniform implementation with the following properties.*\n\n\n1.  *A pricing call may return <span class=\"nodecor\"><span class=\"smallcaps\">Low</span></span>, but it cannot do so if there is a nonzero circulation $d$ satisfying $$\\label{engine:high-direction}\n                        g^{\\mathsf T}d\\ge\\tfrac12\\|\\lambda d\\|_1,$$ unless it reports a detected failure or suspends.*\n\n\n2.  *A call not returning <span class=\"nodecor\"><span class=\"smallcaps\">Low</span></span>, failure, or suspension performs an exact increment $h\\in\\varepsilon\\mathbb Z^E$ that is a circulation and satisfies $$\\label{engine:step-guarantee}\n           \\|\\lambda h\\|_1\\le q/100,\n           \\qquad g^{\\mathsf T}h\\ge q^2/1000.$$ It leaves all logarithmic arguments positive and increases $\\Phi$ by $\\Omega(q^2)$.*\n\n\n3.  *The implementation exposes registers $\\widehat f_e$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-flat-cycle",
        "exact_quote": "Let $M=2^u$, where $u$ is divisible by $2a^2$, and suppose $$\\label{engine:backend-size-range}\n                         S^{1/a}\\le M\\le S^2.$$ Consider a dynamic undirected graph with reference orientations and fixed vertex slots, of total current size at most $M$ and maximum degree at most three. It has positive integral lengths $\\ell_e$ and integral signed gradients $\\gamma_e$, both of magnitude at most $M^{1200a}$. Insertions and deletions are arbitrary; a price replacement is a deletion followed by an insertion. Put $$\\mathop{\\mathrm{OPT}}=\\max_{d\\ne0,\\ d\\text{ a circulation}}\n          \\frac{|\\gamma^{\\mathsf T}d|}{\\|\\ell d\\|_1},$$ with value zero if there is no nonzero circulation.*\n\n*The backend exposes a forest. Every forest vertex maps to an input vertex slot; every forest edge either maps to a current input edge with specified endpoint correspondence, or is null and joins equal images. The multiplicity over each input slot is at most $M^{10/a}/4$. A query returns a list of oriented single input edges and oriented forest paths, the latter specified by endpoints. Each piece is used with unit multiplicity, with repeated pieces listed separately. Their signed physical projection is a circulation $c$. Its *raw length* $V$ is the sum of input lengths over all mapped edge traversals with repetition; a null forest traversal contributes zero. If $\\mathop{\\mathrm{OPT}}>0$, the answer has $V>0$ and $$\\label{engine:flat-quality}\n                   \\frac{|\\gamma^{\\mathsf T}c|}{V}\n                       \\ge M^{-10/a}\\mathop{\\mathrm{OPT}}.$$ An empty or missing answer is permitted when $\\mathop{\\mathrm{OPT}}=0$. There are $M^{O(1/a)+o_a(1)}$ pieces in an answer.*\n\n*Initialization costs $M^{1+O(1/a)+o_a(1)}$. Amortized edit and query costs, including explicitly exposed forest logs, are $M^{O(1/a)+o_a(1)}$, with bounds on every path. Retained forest occurrences and edges have stable lifetimes. Endpoint or mapping changes are replacements, and removals are exposed before the old mappings or input data are invalidated. Transactions permit deletion-first old-to-final updates with no intervening path query.*\n\n*The bounds apply over the polynomial lifespans required by Lemma <a href=\"#lem:priced-steps\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:priced-steps\">21</a>, and against adaptive clients. Reliability can be chosen so that detected failure in an entire engine instance has conditional probability at most $2^{-\\sigma}S^{-60}$. A flagged state has no quality requirement. Deterministic prerequisite checks may suspend, with eventual adequacy as in Lemma <a href=\"#lem:priced-steps\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:priced-steps\">21</a>.",
        "source_id": "PM-120.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-flat-cycle",
          "exact_quote": "Let $M=2^u$, where $u$ is divisible by $2a^2$, and suppose $$\\label{engine:backend-size-range}\n                         S^{1/a}\\le M\\le S^2.$$ Consider a dynamic undirected graph with reference orientations and fixed vertex slots, of total current size at most $M$ and maximum degree at most three. It has positive integral lengths $\\ell_e$ and integral signed gradients $\\gamma_e$, both of magnitude at most $M^{1200a}$. Insertions and deletions are arbitrary; a price replacement is a deletion followed by an insertion. Put $$\\mathop{\\mathrm{OPT}}=\\max_{d\\ne0,\\ d\\text{ a circulation}}\n          \\frac{|\\gamma^{\\mathsf T}d|}{\\|\\ell d\\|_1},$$ with value zero if there is no nonzero circulation.*\n\n*The backend exposes a forest. Every forest vertex maps to an input vertex slot; every forest edge either maps to a current input edge with specified endpoint correspondence, or is null and joins equal images. The multiplicity over each input slot is at most $M^{10/a}/4$. A query returns a list of oriented single input edges and oriented forest paths, the latter specified by endpoints. Each piece is used with unit multiplicity, with repeated pieces listed separately. Their signed physical projection is a circulation $c$. Its *raw length* $V$ is the sum of input lengths over all mapped edge traversals with repetition; a null forest traversal contributes zero. If $\\mathop{\\mathrm{OPT}}>0$, the answer has $V>0$ and $$\\label{engine:flat-quality}\n                   \\frac{|\\gamma^{\\mathsf T}c|}{V}\n                       \\ge M^{-10/a}\\mathop{\\mathrm{OPT}}.$$ An empty or missing answer is permitted when $\\mathop{\\mathrm{OPT}}=0$. There are $M^{O(1/a)+o_a(1)}$ pieces in an answer.*\n\n*Initialization costs $M^{1+O(1/a)+o_a(1)}$. Amortized edit and query costs, including explicitly exposed forest logs, are $M^{O(1/a)+o_a(1)}$, with bounds on every path. Retained forest occurrences and edges have stable lifetimes. Endpoint or mapping changes are replacements, and removals are exposed before the old mappings or input data are invalidated. Transactions permit deletion-first old-to-final updates with no intervening path query.*\n\n*The bounds apply over the polynomial lifespans required by Lemma <a href=\"#lem:priced-steps\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:priced-steps\">21</a>, and against adaptive clients. Reliability can be chosen so that detected failure in an entire engine instance has conditional probability at most $2^{-\\sigma}S^{-60}$. A flagged state has no quality requirement. Deterministic prerequisite checks may suspend, with eventual adequacy as in Lemma <a href=\"#lem:priced-steps\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:priced-steps\">21</a>.",
          "source_id": "PM-120.1"
        }
      ],

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-routing-helper",
        "exact_quote": "Let $F$ be an explicitly listed undirected multigraph with identified edge copies, and let $v_+$ be its number of incident vertex labels. For each edge $f$, an explicit list specifies resource counts $m_{xf}\\in\\{1,\\ldots,10\\}$; unlisted counts are zero. Suppose that $$\\deg_F(v)\\le C_0,\\qquad \\sum_{f\\in F}m_{xf}\\le C_0\n \\quad\\text{for every vertex $v$ and resource $x$},$$ where $C_0\\ge1$ is an integer, degrees count incidences, and both $2|F|$ and the total resource-list length are at most $M^2$. Assume that identifiers and numerical input data have $(\\log S)^{O_a(1)}$ bits. There is a uniform randomized algorithm that either reports detected failure or returns a set $F'\\subseteq F$ and, for every $f=vw\\in F$, an explicit walk $Q_f$ from $v$ to $w$ in $F'$, such that $$\\label{route:guarantees}\n |F'|\\le P_*v_+,\\qquad\n |Q_f|\\le P_*,\\qquad\n \\sum_{f\\in F}\\ \\sum_{e\\text{ traversed in }Q_f}m_{xe}\n       \\le P_*C_0\\quad\\text{for every $x$}.$$ The inner sum counts traversal multiplicities and ignores orientation. A loop demand may be assigned the empty walk. Every returned result is verified. For each fixed reliability parameter $\\sigma\\ge0$, the time on every computation path is $$\\label{route:time}\n (1+\\text{input list size})\\,b\\,M^{o_a(1)},$$ and the probability of detected failure is at most $2^{-\\sigma}S^{-800}$. The same statement holds conditionally when the input is determined by earlier computation and this call uses fresh bits.",
        "source_id": "PM-120.1"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-back-frozen-decomposition",
        "exact_quote": "Fix any state of the epoch and any circulation $\\Delta$ on its current nonloops. Write $\\Delta=\\Delta_s+\\Delta_n$ for its surviving frozen and new coordinates. There are real coefficients $\\alpha_f$ on live fundamental chords and a vector $z$ on the unduplicated raw forests $H_j$ such that, before restricting the frozen coordinates, $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\sum_f\\alpha_f\\pi C_f^0&=\\Delta_s+\\pi z,\n                                      \\label{back:frozen-identity}\\\\\n \\sum_f|\\alpha_f|w_f&\\le A_*\\|\\ell\\Delta_s\\|_1,\n                                      \\label{back:coefficient-bound}\\\\\n \\|z\\|_{\\mathrm{length}}&\\le A_*\\|\\ell\\Delta_s\\|_1.\n                                      \\label{back:raw-residual-bound}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Moreover $z$ is the raw-tree part of $$\\label{back:marginal-cancellation}\n \\sum_v\\bigl(\\operatorname{in}_{\\Delta_s}(v)\n                  -\\operatorname{out}_{\\Delta_s}(v)\\bigr)\\mathbb E[P(v)].$$ Consequently this description uses only upward paths starting at slots incident to new edges. If $z_L$ is the restriction of $z$ to live raw forest edges, then $$\\label{back:clipped-identity}\n             \\sum_f\\alpha_f\\pi C_f^{\\mathrm{clip}}-\\pi z_L\n                                  =\\Delta_s.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-120.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

If $z_L$ is the restriction of $z$ to live raw forest edges, then $$\\label{back:clipped-identity}\n             \\sum_f\\alpha_f\\pi C_f^{\\mathrm{clip}}-\\pi z_L\n                                  =\\Delta_s.$$",

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **matching** ([source](#sec:introduction)): A matching in a graph is a set of edges with pairwise disjoint endpoints.
- **maximum-cardinality matching** ([source](#sec:introduction)): The maximum-cardinality matching problem asks for such a set with the largest possible number of edges.
- **explicit graph input and input-size parameter** ([source](#pm-h-3)): The input is a simple undirected unweighted graph $G=([n],E)$, where $n\ge1$ and $m=\|E\|$. It is given by an array of adjacency-list boundaries and two neighbor occurrences per edge. Labels and indices use $O(\log(n+2))$ bits. The lists may have any valid order. Isolated vertices count toward $n$, and the graph need not be connected. Write $p=n+m$, which is within a constant factor of the number of input word records, and write $\nu(G)$ for the maximum matching size.
- **signed circulation and weighted length norm** ([source](#pm-h-7)): A signed circulation has zero divergence at every vertex; its length norm for positive lengths $\ell$ is $$\\|\ell f\\|_1=\sum_e\ell_e\|f_e\|.$$
- **raw traversal length** ([source](#pm-h-7)): This norm differs from the *raw length* of a supplied traversal list: the latter counts every traversal with its multiplicity, before cancellation.
- **factor-critical bin** ([source](#match:parameters)): A *bin* is a physical odd vertex set with a constructive factor-critical witness: after prescribing any one vertex as its exposure, the others can be matched internally.
- **bin weight** ([source](#match:parameters)): Its weight is $w_d=(\|d\|+1)/2$.
- **rows and assigned-bin injection** ([source](#match:parameters)): The *rows* $\mathcal A$ are physical singleton vertices outside them. An explicit injection $$\mu:\mathcal A\longrightarrow\mathcal D$$ assigns each row along a specified physical edge to its bin.
- **ledger invariant** ([source](#stmt-match-invariant)): There are ledgers $j=0,\ldots,a$, with $c_j=C-j$. Ledger $j$ has nonnegative incidence allocations $x^j_e$, with $$\sum_{e\text{ at row }r}x^j_e=L\qquad(r\in\mathcal A).$$ Its column residual and nonnegative debt are $$y^j_d=L-\operatorname{load}^j_d,\qquad \operatorname{load}^j_d=\sum_{e\text{ at bin }d}x^j_e,\qquad p^j_d\ge0,$$ and they satisfy $$y^j_d+p^j_d\ge c_jw_d. \label{match:ledger-floor}$$ At the top level, $$x_e=x^0_e>0,\qquad s_d=y^0_d+p^0_d-Cw_d>0 \label{match:top-slack}$$ are $\varepsilon$-grid coordinates. The other ledgers are integral. At each settled main-loop state, the bottom debt $p^a$ is zero.
- **certified score** ([source](#pm-h-50)): A *certified score* is a nonnegative rational lower bound on the absolute gradient divided by the positive raw length of a returned traversal list.
- **prescribed-degree factor** ([source](#sec:f-factors)): Recall that an *$f$-factor* of $G=([n],E)$ is a set $F\subseteq E$ with $\deg_F(v)=f(v)$ for every vertex $v$.

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-physical-word-width | physical word length | = | $\lceil c_0\log_2(n+2)\rceil$ | The physical word constant is fixed with the algorithm. |
| q-main-every-path-instructions | maximum-matching instruction count | ≤ | $C p^{1+\eta(p)}$ | A common nonincreasing exponent envelope tends to zero for one uniform program. |
| q-main-success-probability | probability of explicit maximum matching | ≥ | $2/3$ | The probability guarantee holds for every valid input. |
| q-static-integral-flow-time | exact integral minimum-cost-flow time | = | $P^{1+o(1)}$ | The integral-magnitude exponent is fixed. |
| q-capacity-scaling-call-count | polynomial-capacity maximum-flow calls | = | $b$ | The capacity bit length controls the digit rounds. |
| q-phase-gain-target | phase gain target | = | $g_*=\max\left\{1,\left\lfloor\frac{K}{100C}\right\rfloor\right\}$ | The ledger parameter is fixed during the branch. |
| q-phase-row-allocation | row allocation amount | = | $\left\lceil\frac{10000CN}{K}\right\rceil$ | Computed from physical vertex count, positive deficit, and the fixed ledger parameter. |
| q-common-dyadic-grid | top-ledger and engine grid scale | = | $S^{-3000}$ | The master size is a power of two. |
| q-local-projection-time | local exact projection total time | = | $(P+B)S^{O(1/a)+o_a(1)}$ | Fixed sufficiently large parameters and sufficiently large master size. |
| q-subcubic-false-negative-probability | erroneous negative-answer probability | ≤ | $2^{-\sigma}S^{-5}$ | Fresh randomness and valid prerequisites for adaptive invocations; suspensions are excluded from the event being bounded. |
| q-subcubic-every-path-time | fixed-parameter subcubic total time | = | $S^{1+O(1/a)+o_a(1)}$ | Fixed sufficiently large hierarchy parameter and fixed admissible reliability parameter. |
| q-priced-step-weighted-motion | weighted length of an accepted circulation increment | ≤ | $q/100$ | The increment is on the common exact grid. |
| q-priced-step-directional-gain | directional derivative gain of an accepted increment | ≥ | $q^2/1000$ | The call returns a step rather than LOW, detected failure, or suspension. |
| q-engine-total-time | priced-step implementation total time excluding client work | = | $(R+B+Q)S^{O(1/a)+o_a(1)}$ | Fixed parameters, permitted external-operation count, and valid size and precision guards. |
| q-flat-cycle-quality | raw-length circulation score | ≥ | $M^{-10/a}\mathop{\mathrm{OPT}}$ | The score uses raw mapped traversals before cancellation. |
| q-factor-reduction-size | auxiliary factor graph vertices plus edges | = | $O(m\log(n+2))$ | The auxiliary graph may be empty. |
| q-factor-reduction-work-and-records | factor reduction instructions and records | = | $O(n+m\log(n+2))$ | Includes reading degree records and isolated vertices. |
| q-factor-success-probability | probability of returning a prescribed-degree factor when one exists | ≥ | $2/3$ | Infeasible inputs always return NO. |
