<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-static-integral-flow** [claim](#stmt-lem-integral-flow) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-integral-flow): Invoke the deterministic integral-flow theorem, charge its fixed-point simulation, and obtain maximum flow through a bounded return arc. -> Integral arc vector and residual cut certificate.
- **step-small-total-violation** [claim](#stmt-prelim-small-violation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prelim-small-violation): Compare conserved real cut sums with integral lower and upper bounds; a positive integral discrepancy contradicts the strict total-violation margin. -> Existence of an exactly feasible integral circulation.
- **step-capacity-digit-scaling** [claim](#stmt-lem-capacity-scaling) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-capacity-scaling): Double the preceding flow in each binary capacity round and truncate residual capacities using the old cut's augmentation bound. -> Maximum flow using a single fixed polynomial-capacity implementation.
- **step-clocked-fallback** [claim](#stmt-lem-fallback) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-fallback): Deduplicate endpoint pairs while retaining an original identifier, run deterministic maximum matching, and check any perfect witness. -> Exact perfect-matching decision with a fixed polynomial clock.
- **step-bottom-surplus** [claim](#stmt-match-bottom-surplus) / [proof](#proof-stmt-match-bottom-surplus): Count the full row allocations captive inside a bin set and apply the debt-free bottom floor. -> Strict Hall surplus and tight-set weight bound.
- **step-deletion-debt** [claim](#stmt-match-deletion) / [proof](#proof-stmt-match-deletion): Redirect surviving row mass to assigned edges, compensate with debt, and count removed rows and unmatched exposures. -> Exterior invariant, net-debt bound, and matching-count preservation rules.
- **step-top-positivity-maintenance** [claim](#stmt-match-top-maintenance) / [proof](#proof-stmt-match-top-maintenance): Transfer tiny allocations from a bounded-degree donor, repair dirty slacks, and reanchor exact grid values. -> Positive arguments and safe cached expansion thresholds.
- **step-exchange-flow-projection** [claim](#stmt-match-exchange-projection) / [proof](#proof-stmt-match-exchange-projection): Apply an integral exchange flow; on incomplete saturation, take all residual-reachable bins and restrict the updated allocations after their peel. -> Debt-free requested floor or a debt-paying residual peel.
- **step-tight-contraction-witness** [claim](#stmt-match-tight-contraction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-match-tight-contraction): Apply Hall's forbidden-bin criterion and submodularity of deficiency; lift captive-row assignments through the child-bin witnesses. -> Joint freeing or the unique minimal constructively factor-critical tight block.
- **step-bounded-pair-search** [claim](#stmt-match-pair-action) / [proof](#proof-stmt-match-pair-action): Use capped capacity-one witness searches, refine a discovered tight set by residual reachability, and otherwise use loop-erased reverse walks for joint freeing. -> Checked gain or contraction with bounded conditional premature-negative probability.
- **step-materialize-contraction-tree** [claim](#stmt-match-materialization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-match-materialization): Pass prescribed physical exposures down stored child records and repair captive assignments locally before visiting each child once. -> Explicit internal matching within the dissolved physical-size bound.
- **step-local-threshold-soundness** [claim](#stmt-proj-threshold) / [proof](#proof-stmt-proj-threshold): Include return-arc violation in the integral-feasibility argument, preserve sink locks during comparison augmentation, and certify success with true boundary capacities. -> Sound LOW exclusion and true-capacity threshold certificate.
- **step-full-local-residual-cut** [claim](#stmt-proj-local-cut) / [proof](#proof-stmt-proj-local-cut): Use the optimistic minimum source side to identify a full-network cut and solve the true-capacity union before computing local residual reachability. -> Exact full exchange flow and full residual-reachable bin set.
- **step-assemble-local-projection** [claim](#stmt-lem-projection) / [proof](#proof-stmt-proj-local-cut-2): List indexed debts, settle the zero-deficit case directly, binary-search verified thresholds, retain the necessary regions, and apply sparse flow-support changes. -> Local exact projection with every-path cost and explicit failure or suspension outcomes.
- **step-phase-structural-accounting** [claim](#stmt-phase-accounting) / [proof](#proof-stmt-phase-accounting): Charge peels to top debt supplied by repaired gains, then count vertex returns, expansions, bin births, and contraction arities. -> Guarded-prefix bounds for erased weight, blockers, structure, and ticks.
- **step-exclude-perfect-matching-stall** [claim](#stmt-phase-stall) / [proof](#proof-stmt-phase-stall): Compare with vertex-disjoint augmenting paths, free contact bins conceptually, clip overloaded columns, and complete a feasible comparison allocation. -> A high-ascent circulation direction ruling out a valid LOW stall.
- **step-pm-guard-adequacy** [claim](#stmt-phase-guard-adequacy) / [proof](#proof-stmt-phase-guard-adequacy): Analyze a prefix through a hypothetical first interruption, combining structural accounting, objective progress, local locks, and notification charges. -> Eventual deterministic guard adequacy on every path without a matchability promise.
- **step-subcubic-perfect-matching** [claim](#stmt-thm-subcubic) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-subcubic): Run deficit-reducing phases, materialize at boundaries, check final edge coverage, and sum conditional errors over guarded invocations. -> Uniform fixed-parameter subcubic search with checked positives and every-path asymptotic cost.
- **step-augmented-tree-operations** [claim](#stmt-engine-tree-cost) / [proof](#proof-stmt-engine-tree-cost): Maintain signed and absolute lazy tags on subdivided preferred paths and amortize splay access, represented-tree changes, and witnessed threshold reports. -> Logarithmic amortized tree-operation and reporting costs.
- **step-register-drift-accounting** [claim](#stmt-engine-refresh-bound) / [proof](#proof-stmt-engine-refresh-bound): Transfer dying copies' signed and absolute history, coalesce variable flags, and reset all absolute locations before replacement logs. -> Accurate registers and motion-charged refresh and disable bounds.
- **step-priced-step-interface** [claim](#stmt-lem-priced-steps) / [proof](#proof-stmt-engine-refresh-bound-2): Reduce degree through port paths, compute exact integer prices, accept a raw-length-certified backend direction, and apply a compatible dyadic circulation step. -> Priced-step progress, positivity, notification, time, and reliability interface.
- **step-sampling-cut-resource-bounds** [claim](#stmt-route-sampling) / [proof](#proof-stmt-route-sampling): Apply normalized rank-one matrix concentration to cuts, and scalar concentration to sample counts and sparse resource totals. -> Simultaneous cut approximation and depth-conditioned resource-load estimates.
- **step-median-overlay-expansion** [claim](#stmt-route-overlay) / [proof](#proof-stmt-route-overlay): Project averaging rows onto fresh sign vectors, separate median halves, and convert reverse-round mass escape into overlay conductance. -> Regular overlay with the stated probabilistic conductance guarantee.
- **step-lazy-multigraph-mixing** [claim](#stmt-route-cheeger) / [proof](#proof-stmt-route-cheeger): Integrate cut boundaries over threshold sets, use a weighted median and Dirichlet quotient, then bound pointwise error from spectral contraction. -> Lazy spectral gap and pointwise mixing estimates.
- **step-group-conditioned-router** [claim](#stmt-route-router) / [proof](#proof-stmt-route-router): Route independent packet and virtual-job streams through shrinking label groups; prove child invariants, edge-step loads, and substitution bounds with capped rejection. -> Explicit bounded-congestion routes and unconditional failed-run caps.
- **step-static-resource-sparsifier** [claim](#stmt-lem-routing-helper) / [proof](#proof-stmt-route-router-2): Combine halving stages, sampled cut recursion, integral stub flow, median overlays, grouped routing, and filtering; verify endpoints, support, hops, and resources before acceptance. -> Verified representative support and routes with every-path construction cost.
- **step-bottom-cycle-tables** [claim](#stmt-back-base) / [proof](#proof-stmt-back-base): Test dyadic ratio thresholds with exact minimum-cost walk tables, retain predecessor witnesses, and decompose circulations into oriented cycles. -> Explicit circulation with a certified approximate ratio.
- **step-geometric-shift-covers** [claim](#stmt-back-covers) / [proof](#proof-stmt-back-covers): Generate capped geometric delays, compute the best distinct centers by multi-source shortest paths, and verify radius and padding margins. -> Verified padded cell partitions and shortest-path trees.
- **step-bit-selected-forest-family** [claim](#stmt-back-forest-family) / [proof](#proof-stmt-back-forest-family): Enumerate bit-dependent partition choices at each layer and use upward outdegree to certify that each resulting representation is a forest. -> Finite forest family realizing the prescribed pairs of OUT options.
- **step-origin-fixed-route-coupling** [claim](#stmt-back-path-law) / [proof](#proof-stmt-back-path-law): Normalize boundary-distance cell weights, maximally couple endpoint choices layer by layer, and trim the common final suffix. -> Fixed endpoint marginals and expected closing-walk length bound.
- **step-frozen-clipped-decomposition** [claim](#stmt-back-frozen-decomposition) / [proof](#proof-stmt-back-frozen-decomposition): Decompose each coupled edge walk into family fundamental cycles, take finite expectations, collect fixed-marginal imbalances, and restrict dead coordinates. -> Exact frozen and clipped identities with coefficient and raw-remainder norm bounds.
- **step-postorder-portals** [claim](#stmt-back-portals) / [proof](#proof-stmt-back-portals): Accumulate unmarked subtree counts and mark a portal when the prescribed pocket threshold is reached. -> Portal set with disjoint size charges and small remaining components.
- **step-terminal-steiner-compression** [claim](#stmt-back-compression) / [proof](#proof-stmt-back-compression): Sum divergences on terminal-free sides and use zero divergence at suppressed internal nodes. -> Exact gradient and length-norm preservation under compression.
- **step-one-level-quality** [claim](#stmt-lem-backend-reduction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-backend-reduction): Subtract local corrections, compress the raw remainder, and route copy imbalances to physical hubs through null connectors. -> Simultaneous parent-to-local-and-child circulation-ratio inequality.
- **step-class-splits-and-leg-maintenance** [claim](#stmt-update-classes) / [proof](#proof-stmt-update-classes): Maintain nearest-portal regions and depth-band clusters, using fresh labels for both sides of each split or activation. -> Bounded logical edit units and precise leg-change invalidation.
- **step-anchor-core-rebuild** [claim](#stmt-update-anchor-core) / [proof](#proof-stmt-update-anchor-core): Mark old ancestors visited by changed paths, retain live prefixes and suffixes, and sparsify the anchor-core walks with class-visit resources. -> Restored simple path assignments with limited added central chords.
- **step-support-prefix-accounting** [claim](#stmt-update-support) / [proof](#proof-stmt-update-support): Sum rebuild interval counts at each inner period and retain previously selected live support chords. -> Cumulative support and local assignment cost over every epoch prefix.
- **step-compressed-child-maintenance** [claim](#stmt-update-compressed-edits) / [proof](#proof-stmt-update-compressed-edits): Promote persistent terminals, split local segments, promote hole endpoints before cutting, and attach selected chords at their original tree nodes. -> Small bounded-degree child graph with sparse edits and inherited integral prices.
- **step-public-arm-forest-lift** [claim](#stmt-update-flat-lift) / [proof](#proof-stmt-update-flat-lift): Prebuild arm pools, attach child connectors, and replace concatenated realizations by the unique public forest path through endpoint lookup. -> Circulation-preserving lift with no greater raw length and bounded piece count.
- **step-recursive-score-induction** [claim](#stmt-back-quality-induction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-back-quality-induction): Compare local, loop, and child certified scores, applying the quality loss only once at each recursive level. -> Certified level scores and outer approximation guarantee.
- **step-occurrence-pool-bound** [claim](#stmt-update-multiplicity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-update-multiplicity): Count arm interiors and endpoints separately, including dormant pools, and absorb the per-level logarithmic factor into the pool gap. -> Per-input-slot public occurrence bound.
- **step-old-to-final-forest-recourse** [claim](#stmt-update-recourse) / [proof](#proof-stmt-update-recourse): Process child logs passively, rebuild pools only at affected labels, reuse dormant slots elsewhere, and publish deletion-first old-to-final transactions. -> Explicit recourse bound with persistent unaffected mapped-edge lifetimes.
- **step-recursive-work-recurrences** [claim](#stmt-update-recurrences) / [proof](#proof-stmt-update-recurrences): Separate local arm reconstruction from logarithmic child-log processing, charge epoch rebuilds, and unroll the small child edit multiplier. -> Every-prefix work and public-log bounds with absolute exponent coefficients.
- **step-backend-finite-guards-and-error** [claim](#stmt-update-reliability) / [proof](#proof-stmt-update-reliability): Incrementally check capacities, multiplicity, prices, and invocation counts; apply guarded-prefix recurrences and fresh conditional sampling budgets. -> Eventual every-path guard adequacy and conditional engine failure allowance.
- **step-flat-cycle-backend** [claim](#stmt-lem-flat-cycle) / [proof](#proof-stmt-update-reliability-2): Assemble the selected paths, joint child, score induction, public lift, immutable forest logs, work recurrences, and finite reliability checks. -> Complete dynamic flat-cycle interface used by the pricing engine.
- **step-permutation-network** [claim](#stmt-uniform-network) / [proof](#proof-stmt-uniform-network): Build disjoint recursive upper and lower networks and color the input-pair/output-pair permutation cycles alternately. -> Uniform linearithmic network admitting every disjoint permutation routing.
- **step-cardinality-network-gadget** [claim](#stmt-uniform-cardinality) / [proof](#proof-stmt-uniform-cardinality): Split network positions, attach original vertices and mandatory sinks, and count externally matched input and output endpoints. -> Equivalent perfect-matching instance and explicit original-edge decoder.
- **step-subcubic-path-gadgets** [claim](#stmt-uniform-degree) / [proof](#proof-stmt-uniform-degree): Detect isolates first, then replace each vertex by an odd path with external incidences at successive odd positions. -> Equivalent maximum-degree-three instance and bidirectional witness conversion.
- **step-fixed-branch-performance** [claim](#stmt-uniform-fixed-branch) / [proof](#proof-stmt-uniform-fixed-branch): Combine witness reductions, binary-search thresholds, and eventual guarded-prefix adequacy at a sufficiently large fixed branch index. -> Every-path fixed-branch near-linear instruction bound.
- **step-finite-physical-schedule** [claim](#stmt-uniform-physical-ram) / [proof](#proof-stmt-uniform-physical-ram): Dovetail interruptible instructions, let the polynomial fallback bound total executed quotas and allocations, and choose the physical word constant from that bound. -> A single finite program halting on every path with polynomial physical addressability.
- **step-uniform-main-envelope** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-uniform-physical-ram-2): Compete with every fixed branch through instruction quotas, sum branch error allowances without conditioning on the winner, and define a common nonincreasing supremum envelope. -> One uniform every-path almost-linear algorithm and explicit maximum matching with the stated success probability.
- **step-factor-incidence-network-reduction** [claim](#stmt-factor-reduction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-factor-reduction): Create incidence ports and host-labeled cross edges, install separate split permutation networks with local mandatory sinks, and decode selected cross-edge labels. -> Equivalent simple perfect-matching graph and factor decoder with logarithmic incidence expansion.
- **step-uniform-factor-search** [claim](#stmt-cor-f-factor) / [proof](#proof-stmt-factor-reduction-2): Handle the empty-edge case, run uniform maximum matching on the reduction, check perfect coverage and prescribed host degrees, and simulate virtual words with one fixed physical width. -> Almost-linear uniform factor search with checked positive outputs and one-sided decision error.

          "exact_quote": "Apply the preceding coupled-walk decomposition separately to each surviving frozen edge, weighted by its signed coefficient in $\\Delta_s$, and take expectations. There are finitely many choices in these conceptual distributions.",
          "source_id": "PM-120.1"
        }
      ],
      "reason": "Real coefficients and the raw comparison vector arise from finite conceptual expectations, rather than an algorithm for evaluating the decomposition.",
      "step_id": "step-frozen-clipped-decomposition"
    },
    {
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-backend-reduction",
          "exact_quote": "Every distribution, coupling, coefficient, and comparison vector in this proof is existential. The algorithm constructs only integral graphs, terminal sets, path assignments, and rational scores.",
          "source_id": "PM-120.1"
        }
      ],
      "reason": "The quality proof uses existential distributions, coefficients, and comparison vectors; the executed construction maintains integral graphs and rational candidate scores.",
      "step_id": "step-one-level-quality"
    },
    {
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "proof-stmt-phase-stall",
          "exact_quote": "For this argument only, realize the matching represented by the partition. This is an existence comparison, not an instruction to materialize the graph at every pricing step.",
          "source_id": "PM-120.1"
        }
      ],
      "reason": "Materialization and comparison with an existing perfect matching are existence arguments for ascent, not instructions to expand the matching at every pricing step.",
      "step_id": "step-exclude-perfect-matching-stall"
    },
    {
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "proof-stmt-uniform-physical-ram-2",
          "exact_quote": "To exhibit one envelope, let $T(G,\\omega)$ be the actual total instruction count of the schedule on input $G$ and random path $\\omega$. For $x\\ge2$ define $$e(x)=\\sup_{\\substack{G,\\omega:\\,n(G)+m(G)\\ge x}}\n \\max\\left\\{0,\n \\log_{n(G)+m(G)}\\bigl(\\max\\{1,T(G,\\omega)\\}\\bigr)-1\\right\\}.$$ The polynomial fallback bounds this supremum, and the preceding every-path estimates imply $e(x)\\to0$. Put $\\eta(q)=e(\\max\\{2,q\\})$ for integers $q\\ge1$.",
          "source_id": "PM-120.1"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "pm-h-5",
          "exact_quote": "Finally, instruction-level dovetailing with summable error budgets turns all fixed-parameter guarantees into Theorem <a href=\"#thm:main\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:main\">1</a>, without a convergence rate or an input-dependent table of thresholds.",
          "source_id": "PM-120.1"
        }
      ],
      "reason": "The common envelope is defined by a supremum over all sufficiently large graph inputs and random paths. The proof supplies no encoded threshold table or convergence rate to be used by the program.",
      "step_id": "step-uniform-main-envelope"
    }
  ],
  "pm_id": "PM-120.1",
  "schema": "pm.roadmap.v2",
  "steps": [
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-integral-flow",
      "depends_on": [],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-integral-flow",
          "exact_quote": "For an explicitly represented directed network of total size at most $P$, with integral capacities, demands and costs bounded in magnitude by a fixed power of $P$, exact integral minimum-cost flow can be computed uniformly and deterministically in $P^{1+o(1)}$ time. Its finite-precision operations can be simulated with polynomially logarithmic overhead on logarithmic-bit words. In particular, integral source–sink maximum flow with nonnegative upper capacities and zero lower capacities returns an explicit integral arc vector and a residual-reachability minimum-cut certificate within that bound.",
          "source_id": "PM-120.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-static-integral-flow",
      "inputs": [
        "Explicit network with integral polynomial-magnitude data"
      ],
      "method": "Invoke the deterministic integral-flow theorem, charge its fixed-point simulation, and obtain maximum flow through a bounded return arc.",
      "output": "Integral arc vector and residual cut certificate.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-integral-flow"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-prelim-small-violation",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-static-integral-flow"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prelim-small-violation",
          "exact_quote": "Let every lower and upper arc bound be integral, with lower bound no larger than upper bound. If an exactly conserved real arc vector has total bound violation less than one, the bounds admit an exactly feasible integral circulation.",
          "source_id": "PM-120.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-small-total-violation",
      "inputs": [
        "Integral arc bounds",
        "Exactly conserved real vector"
      ],
      "method": "Compare conserved real cut sums with integral lower and upper bounds; a positive integral discrepancy contradicts the strict total-violation margin.",
      "output": "Existence of an exactly feasible integral circulation.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-prelim-small-violation"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-capacity-scaling",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-static-integral-flow"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-capacity-scaling",
          "exact_quote": "For maximum flow with nonnegative integral upper capacities of at most $b$ bits, a single fixed polynomial-capacity version of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:integral-flow\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:integral-flow\">2</a> suffices, using $b$ calls on linear-size residual graphs, together with the charged capacity arithmetic and identifier translations.",
          "source_id": "PM-120.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-capacity-digit-scaling",
      "inputs": [
        "Nonnegative integral upper capacities"
      ],
      "method": "Double the preceding flow in each binary capacity round and truncate residual capacities using the old cut's augmentation bound.",
      "output": "Maximum flow using a single fixed polynomial-capacity implementation.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-capacity-scaling"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-fallback",
      "depends_on": [],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-fallback",
          "exact_quote": "There is one uniform deterministic algorithm on explicit loopless graphs of maximum degree three that halts within a fixed polynomial number of finite-word instructions. It returns a checked perfect matching when one exists and a negative answer otherwise.",
          "source_id": "PM-120.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-clocked-fallback",
      "inputs": [
        "Explicit loopless subcubic graph"
      ],
      "method": "Deduplicate endpoint pairs while retaining an original identifier, run deterministic maximum matching, and check any perfect witness.",
      "output": "Exact perfect-matching decision with a fixed polynomial clock.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-fallback"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-match-bottom-surplus",
      "depends_on": [],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-match-bottom-surplus",
          "exact_quote": "At each settled state and every nonempty $X\\subseteq\\mathcal D$, $$Lh(X)\\ge y^a(X)\\ge w(X)>0.\n \\label{match:bottom-certificate}$$ Thus $h(X)\\ge1$, every bin has weight at most $L$, and a tight set $h(X)=1$ has total weight at most $L$.",
          "source_id": "PM-120.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-bottom-surplus",
      "inputs": [
        "Ledger invariant",
        "Assigned-row injection"
      ],
      "method": "Count the full row allocations captive inside a bin set and apply the debt-free bottom floor.",
      "output": "Strict Hall surplus and tight-set weight bound.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-match-bottom-surplus"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-match-deletion",
      "depends_on": [],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-match-deletion",
          "exact_quote": "In a preserved ledger of floor $cw$, redirect each allocation of a surviving row into $R$ to its current assigned edge, adding an equal debt at the recipient bin. This preserves the exterior ledger invariant. If $O$ is redirected mass, $I$ is removed rows’ allocation to the exterior, and $f$ is the number of free bins removed, then $$\\begin{split}\n O&=I+Lf-y(R),\\\\\n I&\\le L(h(R)-f),\\\\\n \\Delta\\!\\left(\\sum_d p_d\\right)=O-p(R)&\\le Lh(R)-cw(R).\n \\end{split}\n \\label{match:deletion-debt}$$ The last bound is at most $2L$ at a gain. A gain decreases the represented unmatched count by two; contractions, expansions, and peels leave it unchanged.",
          "source_id": "PM-120.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-deletion-debt",
      "inputs": [
        "Preserved ledger",
        "Bin deletion set"
      ],
      "method": "Redirect surviving row mass to assigned edges, compensate with debt, and count removed rows and unmatched exposures.",
      "output": "Exterior invariant, net-debt bound, and matching-count preservation rules.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-match-deletion"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-match-top-maintenance",
      "depends_on": [],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-match-top-maintenance",
          "exact_quote": "The top ledger can be maintained on the $\\varepsilon$-grid with logarithmic arguments above $\\delta$ before pricing and above $\\delta/10$ at refreshes. Apart from structural work, repairs cost constantly many explicit point operations per dirty coordinate. Between structural operations a small-allocation repair decreases $\\Phi$ by at most $O(\\delta)$, and slack repairs never decrease it. Positivity repairs add less than $S^{-800}$ debt on every guarded phase prefix. Moreover, $$\\begin{split}\n \\widehat s_d\\ge4C&\\ \\Longrightarrow\\ s_d\\ge3C,\\\\\n \\text{no eligible expansion}&\\ \\Longrightarrow\\\n s_d\\le5C\\quad\\text{for every bin with a \\(U\\)-contact}.\n \\end{split}\n \\label{match:contact-slacks}$$",
          "source_id": "PM-120.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-top-positivity-maintenance",
      "inputs": [
        "Top ledger",
        "Dirty register notifications"
      ],
      "method": "Transfer tiny allocations from a bounded-degree donor, repair dirty slacks, and reanchor exact grid values.",
      "output": "Positive arguments and safe cached expansion thresholds.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-match-top-maintenance"
    },

In the latter case, $$Lh(R)<c_jw(R).\n \\label{match:failed-floor}$$ Every preserved coarser ledger loses at least $w(R)$ in net debt, and the number $f$ of new blockers satisfies $$f\\le h(R)<\\frac{Cw(R)}{L}.\n \\label{match:peel-blockers}$$",
          "source_id": "PM-120.1"

Either the injection can be rearranged to leave both free, or a unique inclusion-minimal $X\\supseteq\\{i,j\\}$ satisfies $h(X)=1$. In the latter case $w(X)\\le L$, and the physical block $\\bigl(\\bigcup_{d\\in X}d\\bigr)\\cup A(X)$ is constructively factor-critical using the joining edge, its row–bin incidences, and its child-bin witnesses.",

Continued contractions cost $O(|X|\\mathop{\\mathrm{polylog}}S)$; a gain attempt or final abort costs $O(L\\mathop{\\mathrm{polylog}}S)$, with fixed-parameter reliability factors. These work bounds hold on every path.",

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **invariant-portal-budget-and-pockets**: The constructed portal set respects the forest-size budget, and every component left after removing the portals has fewer than the prescribed threshold of vertices.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-cyclic-portal-input**: Apply the postorder portal construction to an arbitrary graph after choosing a spanning forest. -> Edges omitted from the spanning forest can reconnect the remaining pockets, invalidating the component-size guarantee in the original graph.
- **anti-rounded-up-portal-budget**: Permit an additional portal by rounding the forest-size budget upward. -> An enlarged budget does not establish the stated bound; the proof pays for every mark with a disjoint group of sufficiently many vertices.
- **anti-global-matching-certification**: Treat successful portal tests as certification of the almost-linear maximum-matching algorithm. -> Portal selection verifies a local forest construction; it does not verify the matching state, circulation backend, randomized schedule, or claimed instruction envelope.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. The portal-grid lemma gives a finite construction: The set is constructed by one postorder traversal. This implementation and oracle check only its portal-budget and remaining-component conclusions, not the full matching algorithm or its asymptotic running time.

```python
# INPUTS: JSON object with n, k, edges, and an optional case label.
# PRECONDITIONS: 0 <= n <= 8; 1 <= k <= 8; edges describe a simple undirected forest on range(n).
# OUTPUTS: JSON object containing a sorted list of portal vertex labels.
# TIME: Linear in the forest representation, excluding serialization overhead.
# SPACE: Linear in the forest representation.
# ARITHMETIC: Exact Python integer counts and comparisons.
# FAILURE: Invalid inputs raise an exception and are not theorem instances.
import json
import sys

def integer(x):
    return type(x) is int

def construct(data):
    n = data['n']
    k = data['k']
    edges = data['edges']
    if not integer(n) or not integer(k) or not (0 <= n <= 8 and 1 <= k <= 8):
        raise ValueError('invalid size or threshold')
    if type(edges) is not list:
        raise ValueError('invalid edge list')
    adj = [[] for _ in range(n)]
    pairs = set()
    for edge in edges:
        if type(edge) is not list or len(edge) != 2:
            raise ValueError('invalid edge')
        u, v = edge
        if not integer(u) or not integer(v) or not (0 <= u < n and 0 <= v < n) or u == v:
            raise ValueError('invalid endpoints')
        pair = (min(u, v), max(u, v))
        if pair in pairs:
            raise ValueError('duplicate edge')
        pairs.add(pair)
        adj[u].append(v)
        adj[v].append(u)
    parent = [-1] * n
    seen = [False] * n
    order = []
    for root in range(n):
        if seen[root]:
            continue
        seen[root] = True
        stack = [root]
        while stack:
            v = stack.pop()
            order.append(v)
            for u in adj[v]:
                if u == parent[v]:
                    continue
                if seen[u]:
                    raise ValueError('cycle')
                seen[u] = True
                parent[u] = v
                stack.append(u)
    counts = [0] * n
    portals = []
    for v in reversed(order):
        total = 1
        for u in adj[v]:
            if parent[u] == v:
                total += counts[u]
        if total >= k:
            portals.append(v)
            counts[v] = 0
        else:
            counts[v] = total
    return {'portals': sorted(portals)}

print(json.dumps(construct(json.load(sys.stdin)), sort_keys=True))
```

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | True | strong | adaptable | inferred | A static compatibility graph can represent eligible pairings between distinct software entities. Maximum-cardinality matching constructs the largest set of pairings that share no endpoint, provided eligibility is expressed by a simple undirected unweighted graph. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The construction solves static edge pairing and prescribed-degree feasibility. The supplied corpus does not construct game dynamics, collision response, or a simulation integrator. |
| tools | True | strong | implementable | inferred | The explicit witness checks support a graph-feasibility tool that validates proposed perfect matchings and decodes accepted auxiliary witnesses into host-graph factors. These checks establish accepted-output validity while preserving the distinction between a negative randomized answer and a proof of infeasibility. |
| algorithms | True | strong | implementable | inferred | The degree-reduction construction explicitly replaces each positive-degree vertex by an incidence path, preserving perfect matchability and allowing witness conversion in both directions. It provides a concrete reduction from loopless perfect matching to subcubic perfect matching. |
| data_structures | True | strong | implementable | inferred | The augmented forest representation maintains oriented path sums, signed coordinate accumulation, absolute traversal motion, and witnessed refresh thresholds. Its stable mapped-edge lifetimes make implicit path updates compatible with exact coordinate recovery through forest replacements. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The instruction guarantee is proved for a classical word-RAM with charged finite-word operations. No accelerator architecture, parallel kernel, or device-memory implementation is constructed. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Fresh fair bits provide algorithmic reliability, and explicit edge checks establish combinatorial witness validity. The corpus supplies no cryptographic primitive, adversarial security experiment, or computational hardness reduction. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The logarithmic objective supplies circulation directions for exact combinatorial search. The construction does not define a learned predictor, a training procedure, or a statistical generalization guarantee. |
| optimisation_operations_research | True | strong | adaptable | inferred | Prescribed-degree factors give a concrete feasibility model for selecting compatible unit incidences with exact quotas.       "pseudocode": "For the singleton case, create one position with its length-zero route. Otherwise create the outer input and output positions and recursively build disjoint upper and lower networks. Connect each outer pair to both recursive copies. For a supplied permutation, form the bipartite multigraph of input pairs and output pairs, alternately color its cycles, route each color through its recursive copy, and attach the outer arcs.",
      "source_evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-uniform-network",
          "exact_quote": "For every power of two $s\\ge1$, there is a directed acyclic network with $s$ distinct input positions and $s$ distinct output positions, of size $O(s\\log(s+2))$, in which every permutation of inputs to outputs is routable by vertex-disjoint directed paths. A position may be both an input and an output when $s=1$. The network can be constructed uniformly within its size bound.",
          "source_object": "stmt-uniform-network"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "proof-stmt-uniform-network",
          "exact_quote": "Given a permutation, form a bipartite multigraph between its input pairs and output pairs, with one edge per assigned input–output pair. Every vertex has degree two, so the components are even cycles, allowing a cycle of two parallel edges.",
          "source_object": "proof-stmt-uniform-network"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "proof-stmt-uniform-network",
          "exact_quote": "Color each cycle alternately upper and lower. Every input pair and output pair has one edge of each color. The upper edges specify a permutation through the upper network, and the lower edges one through the lower network. Route inductively and attach the corresponding outer arcs. The two copies are disjoint and each outer position occurs once, giving vertex-disjoint routes.",
          "source_object": "proof-stmt-uniform-network"
        }
      ],
      "space_complexity": "O(s\\log(s+2)) network size.",
      "strength": "strong",
      "time_complexity": "O(s\\log(s+2)) network construction.",
      "verification": "PENDING",
      "why": "The recursive permutation-network construction gives an explicit finite graph gadget in which every input-output permutation admits vertex-disjoint directed routes. Alternating cycle coloring of paired input and output positions provides the constructive routing argument."
    }
  ],
  "generation_status": "COMPLETE",
  "pm_id": "PM-120.1",
  "schema": "pm.domains.v2"
}

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-explicit-simple-unweighted-input** (formal_scope): The main matching guarantee concerns valid explicit simple undirected unweighted graphs. Directed dependencies, weighted objectives, and implicit graph access require separate results. Blocked use: ['Apply the main instruction bound to a directed precedence graph.', 'Claim minimum-cost pairing from maximum cardinality.', 'Exclude isolated vertices from the input-size accounting.']
- **limit-charged-computational-model** (formal_scope): The instruction guarantee includes random bits, initialization, allocation, data movement, output, and charged longer arithmetic in the stated classical word-RAM. Blocked use: ['Treat arbitrary-precision arithmetic as a unit-cost operation.', 'Transfer the bound directly to accelerator latency or distributed communication rounds.']
- **limit-probabilistic-main-optimality** (approximation): The matching objective is exact maximum cardinality, but the guarantee of returning an optimum is probabilistic. Checking edge membership and endpoint disjointness alone does not certify optimality. Blocked use: ['Declare every returned valid matching maximum.', 'Treat the randomized matching algorithm as an always-correct optimum oracle.']
- **limit-integral-flow-input-magnitudes** (hypothesis): The static integral-flow interface assumes explicit integral data with magnitudes bounded by a fixed power of the represented network size. Larger capacities require the separately charged capacity-scaling construction. Blocked use: ['Use unrestricted capacity magnitudes without charging their arithmetic.', 'Invoke the integral interface on arbitrary real costs.']
- **limit-strict-total-violation-and-conservation** (hypothesis): The integral-feasibility implication requires exact conservation, integral consistent bounds, and total bound violation strictly less than one. It does not assert that the supplied real vector is integral. Blocked use: ['Round an approximately conserved vector and claim exact feasibility.', 'Ignore return-arc violation when checking the total error.', 'Replace the strict total-violation condition by a non-strict condition.']
- **limit-settled-bottom-certificate** (hypothesis): Strict Hall surplus is certified at settled states through the debt-free bottom ledger. Intermediate states with outstanding bottom debt do not inherit that certificate merely from their row assignment. Blocked use: ['Apply settled-state tight-set reasoning before scheduled projection finishes.', 'Infer strict surplus solely from an injective assignment.']
- **limit-constructive-bin-witnesses** (hypothesis): Contraction and materialization rely on child-bin exposure witnesses and stored captive incidences. An arbitrary odd vertex set is not an interchangeable bin. Blocked use: ['Contract an odd block without preserving an internal exposure witness.', 'Decode a matching after discarding the contraction records.']
- **limit-sparse-local-projection-access** (hypothesis): The local projection bound requires an integral higher-floor parent, sparse point access and indexed updates, and the stated debt-size regime. It is not a bound for repeatedly rebuilding the entire parent ledger. Blocked use: ['Replace indexed positive-debt access with an uncharged global scan.', 'Claim debt-local work without the higher parent floor.', 'Copy every ledger coordinate at each reset.']
- **limit-threshold-failure-and-locks** (hypothesis): Threshold soundness is conditional on an unfailed nonsuspended test. Boundary locks, exact conservation, padding budgets, and true-capacity verification belong to the construction and cannot be omitted. Blocked use: ['Interpret a failed pricing attempt as a certified threshold decision.', 'Accept optimistic boundary flow without the true-capacity check.', 'Remove lower locks while retaining the exploration-size accounting.']
- **limit-full-residual-peel-recovery** (hypothesis): Exact peel recovery uses the required success and failure regions with true capacities and original arc identifiers. A threshold value or an arbitrary partial reachable set is insufficient. Blocked use: ['Peel only the bins encountered by a truncated search.', 'Merge local networks after losing original arc identity.', 'Claim full residual reachability from a scalar maximum-flow value.']
- **limit-random-negative-versus-suspension** (approximation): A capped stochastic search can issue an erroneous negative answer. A deterministic prerequisite failure instead suspends the parameter branch without an answer; the false-negative probability does not bound suspensions. Blocked use: ['Publish every randomized negative as a deterministic infeasibility certificate.', 'Count a suspended branch as a negative test.', 'Condition the error allowance on the branch later winning the schedule.']
- **limit-fixed-parameter-asymptotics** (formal_scope): Eventual guard adequacy and the subcubic asymptotic bound hold after fixing sufficiently large parameters. The analysis does not justify increasing the hierarchy parameter with input size while reusing fixed-parameter constants. Blocked use: ['Choose an input-dependent hierarchy parameter from the asymptotic notation alone.', 'Assume every finite input passes every deterministic guard.', 'Infer a numerical crossover threshold absent from the proof.']
- **limit-priced-objective-descriptors** (hypothesis): The priced-step interface uses specified logarithmic descriptors, positive grid arguments, bounded numerical data, and a lifetime bound counting topology births and retargetings. Arbitrary smooth objectives are outside its contract. Blocked use: ['Substitute an arbitrary learned or numerical direction while retaining the priced-step guarantee.', 'Ignore new endpoint slots created by retargeting.', 'Replace exact grid circulation increments by floating-point updates.']
- **limit-raw-motion-before-cancellation** (hypothesis): Raw traversal length and absolute motion count repeated traversals before cancellation. A short net circulation does not justify a smaller motion charge for its supplied representation. Blocked use: ['Erase opposite traversals from refresh accounting.', "Use net coefficient length as the backend's raw-length denominator.", 'Infer cheap execution solely from cancellation in the projected vector.']
- **limit-stable-forest-history** (hypothesis): Exact coordinate recovery requires stable mapped-edge lifetimes, transfers before deletion, and settlement without intervening primal motion. Dormant-slot reassignment does not authorize resetting retained history. Blocked use: ['Invalidate an old map before transferring its saved accumulation.', 'Reset a retained dormant copy when it receives a new occurrence.', 'Issue a path query during an unfinished old-to-final transaction.']
- **limit-routing-resource-contract** (hypothesis): The routing helper assumes explicitly identified demand edges, bounded sparse resource counts, checked size and degree prerequisites, and fresh randomness for adaptive calls. Its accepted walks are verified, while failed attempts provide no routing witness. Blocked use: ['Use arbitrary resource multiplicities without a reduction.', 'Charge a resource once when its edge is traversed repeatedly.', 'Treat an exhausted routing attempt as a valid sparsifier.']
- **limit-existential-route-comparisons** (nonconstructive): The upward route laws, couplings, real coefficients, and comparison vectors are proof objects. They do not supply an executable procedure for evaluating the frozen decomposition. Blocked use: ['Implement the conceptual route distribution as though its probabilities were provided.', 'Claim that the real decomposition coefficients are computed by the backend.', 'Use individual clipped fundamental vectors as current circulations.']
- **limit-terminal-forest-compression** (hypothesis): Exact suppression of forest segments requires the terminal set to contain every divergence location. Missing terminals or additional nonforest edges invalidate the stated compression argument. Blocked use: ['Suppress a vertex carrying unrecorded divergence.', 'Transfer the terminal-free branch argument directly to a cyclic graph.', 'Drop hole endpoints from compression terminals.']
- **limit-portal-certificate-scope** (formal_scope): The executable portal check concerns a finite forest construction and its portal-count and remaining-component conclusions. It does not certify the full randomized matching algorithm, its recursive backend, or its asymptotic instruction envelope. Blocked use: ['Treat portal tests as a certificate of maximum matching.', 'Apply the pocket guarantee to the original cyclic graph after selecting a spanning forest.', 'Infer the general running-time theorem from the finite vector suite.']
- **limit-witness-preserving-reductions** (formal_scope): The cardinality gadget assumes a valid cardinality threshold, and the path gadget preserves perfect matchability after checking isolates. Arbitrary imperfect gadget matchings are not covered by the perfect-witness decoder. Blocked use: ['Use a cardinality threshold outside its stated range.', 'Claim general maximum-cardinality preservation from the degree gadget alone.', 'Decode an unchecked auxiliary matching as a perfect host witness.']
- **limit-uniform-envelope-not-calibration** (nonconstructive): The common exponent envelope is an analytic supremum bound. The uniform program does not receive a convergence rate or an encoded table of adequacy thresholds. Blocked use: ['Present the envelope as a computable performance estimator.', 'Choose a branch using an invented convergence threshold.', 'Replace instruction-level scheduling by uncharged whole-routine scheduling.']
- **limit-valid-exact-factor-degrees** (hypothesis): Factor search requires valid prescribed degrees in a simple host and selects a host-edge subset. It does not encode unrestricted edge multiplicities, weighted costs, or additive sizes of unequal items. Blocked use: ['Model repeatable shipments by repeated selection of a simple host edge.', 'Interpret exact prescribed degrees as upper-capacity inequalities.', 'Claim minimum weighted transportation cost from factor feasibility.']
- **limit-empty-factor-and-positive-checks** (edge_case): The auxiliary factor graph may be empty. Valid edgeless hosts require an empty factor, and every accepted nontrivial factor must pass auxiliary coverage and decoded host-degree checks. Blocked use: ['Reject the empty auxiliary graph as automatic infeasibility.', 'Return selected cross-edge labels without checking host membership and prescribed degrees.', 'Treat a randomized NO answer on a feasible host as certain nonexistence.']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove that maximum-cardinality matching in a simple undirected graph with <i>n</i> vertices and <i>m</i> edges can be found by one uniform randomized algorithm in $`(n+m)^{1+o(1)}`$ time. The bound holds on every computation path in a logarithmic-word model, and the algorithm returns an explicit maximum matching with probability at least 2/3. An explicit reduction gives the same time and probability guarantees for deciding whether a simple host graph has a spanning subgraph with prescribed valid vertex degrees, and for finding one when it exists.

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

A matching in a graph is a set of edges with pairwise disjoint endpoints. The maximum-cardinality matching problem asks for such a set with the largest possible number of edges. We prove an almost-linear bound in the adjacency-list input size for general graphs, with explicit output and a single uniform algorithm. The running-time guarantee includes every unsuccessful random computation, all finite-precision work, and all input and output costs.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### Statement and computational model

The input is a simple undirected unweighted graph $G=([n],E)$, where $n\ge1$ and $m=|E|$. It is given by an array of adjacency-list boundaries and two neighbor occurrences per edge. Labels and indices use $O(\log(n+2))$ bits. The lists may have any valid order. Isolated vertices count toward $n$, and the graph need not be connected. Write $p=n+m$, which is within a constant factor of the number of input word records, and write $\nu(G)$ for the maximum matching size.

We use a classical randomized word-RAM whose word length is $$w=\lceil c_0\log_2(n+2)\rceil,$$ for a fixed constant $c_0\ge2$ chosen with the algorithm. Unit-cost instructions are word reads and writes, comparison, addition, subtraction, multiplication modulo $2^w$, integer quotient and remainder with nonzero divisor, Boolean operations, and shifts by fewer than $w$ positions. Each independent fair random bit costs one instruction. Longer arithmetic uses charged multiword implementations. Allocation, initialization, data movement, preprocessing, and output writing are included. There is no oracle or nonuniform advice. Since $n\le p\le n^2$ for a simple graph, the word length is also $\Theta(\log(p+2))$.

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1** (Exact matching in almost-linear input-size time). *There are a single uniform randomized word-RAM algorithm, fixed constants $c_0\ge2$ and $C\ge1$, and a nonincreasing function $\eta:\mathbb N_{\ge1}\to\mathbb R_{\ge0}$ with $\eta(q)\to0$ as $q\to\infty$ such that the following holds. For every input graph $G=([n],E)$ with $m=|E|$ and $p=n+m$, the algorithm halts on every computation path in at most $$C p^{1+\eta(p)}$$ instructions. With probability at least $2/3$ it outputs an explicit list of edges of a matching of size $\nu(G)$.*

</div>

For every fixed integer $\kappa\ge1$, this gives $C_\kappa n^{1+\eta(n)}$ instructions on graphs with $m\le\kappa n$, for some constant $C_\kappa$. The same algorithm and exponent envelope work for all $\kappa$, which need not be supplied as an input. At the dense extreme $m=\Theta(n^2)$, the bound is $n^{2+o(1)}$.

A perfect matching is not promised. In particular, the perfect-matching procedure for graphs of maximum degree three will be used only after reductions that preserve explicit witnesses. A separate algorithm for each fixed exponent slack would also be insufficient; Section <a href="#sec:uniformization" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:uniformization">9</a> constructs the single finite program and the common envelope in Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>.

A reduction also gives an almost-linear bound for prescribed vertex degrees. Given an integer $f(v)$ with $0\le f(v)\le\deg_G(v)$ for every vertex $v$, an *$f$-factor* is a spanning subgraph $([n],F)$ with $\deg_F(v)=f(v)$ for every $v$. Corollary <a href="#cor:f-factor" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:f-factor">53</a> gives one uniform randomized algorithm that decides whether an $f$-factor exists and returns its edge list when it does, with success probability at least $2/3$ and $(n+m)^{1+o(1)}$ instructions on every computation path. The degree prescription is an array of one-word integers. Section <a href="#sec:f-factors" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:f-factors">10</a> gives the witness-preserving reduction with only logarithmic expansion in the number of edge incidences.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### Context and significance

The structure of matchings has long connected combinatorial existence criteria with efficient construction. Hall’s theorem characterizes when a family of sets has distinct representatives (Hall 1935) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[Hall1935](#ref-Hall1935)], and Tutte’s perfect-matching theorem identifies the obstruction caused by odd components (Tutte 1947) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[Tutte1947](#ref-Tutte1947)]. Berge’s augmenting-path criterion turns maximum cardinality into a question about alternating paths (Berge 1957) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[Berge1957](#ref-Berge1957)]. Edmonds made this approach algorithmic in general graphs through blossom contraction and expansion (Edmonds 1965b) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[EdmondsPaths](#ref-EdmondsPaths)]. His matching-polytope theorem gives a complementary explanation of the odd-set constraints (Edmonds 1965a) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[EdmondsPolytope](#ref-EdmondsPolytope)]: vertex-degree inequalities alone allow, for example, value $1/2$ on each edge of a triangle.

Tutte’s short proof of the factor theorem reduces prescribed-degree $f$-factors to perfect matchings (Tutte 1954) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[Tutte1954Factor](#ref-Tutte1954Factor)]. The reduction in Section <a href="#sec:f-factors" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:f-factors">10</a> follows this incidence-port approach, replacing the local complete connections to auxiliary vertices by the rearrangeable networks used in Section <a href="#sec:uniformization" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:uniformization">9</a>. This controls the size of the perfect-matching instance even when a host vertex has high degree. There are also direct algorithms: Gabow finds a maximum-cardinality edge set subject to $\deg_F(v)\le f(v)$ in $O(n^{2/3}m)$ time on simple graphs (Gabow 2025, Theorem 4.1) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[Gabow2025FMatching](#ref-Gabow2025FMatching)]. After linear-time input processing, including removal of zero-demand vertices, this gives an $O(n+n^{2/3}m)$ factor test: an $f$-factor exists exactly when the returned set has $2|F|=\sum_v f(v)$.

Batching shortest augmenting paths led to the bipartite algorithms of Hopcroft and Karp (Hopcroft and Karp 1973) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[HopcroftKarp1973](#ref-HopcroftKarp1973)] and, independently, Karzanov (Karzanov 1973) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[Karzanov1973Representatives](#ref-Karzanov1973Representatives)], and to the Micali–Vazirani algorithm for general graphs (Micali and Vazirani 1980; Vazirani 2024) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[MicaliVazirani](#ref-MicaliVazirani), [MicaliVaziraniAnalysis](#ref-MicaliVaziraniAnalysis)]. The latter has running time $O(m\sqrt n)$ on a RAM; reading the explicit input and removing isolates gives the convenient universal bound $O(n+m\sqrt n)$. This is $O_\kappa(n^{3/2})$ when $m\le\kappa n$. Gabow and Tarjan developed a different route through weighted scaling (Gabow and Tarjan 1991) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[GabowTarjan1991](#ref-GabowTarjan1991)], and Gabow later gave a direct weighted-search formulation at the same cardinality bound (Gabow 2017) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[Gabow2017](#ref-Gabow2017)]. On very dense graphs, Goldberg and Karzanov improve the combinatorial bound to $O(n+m\sqrt n\log(n^2/m)/\log n)$ by combining skew-symmetric flow with graph compression (Goldberg and Karzanov 2004, Theorem 10.3) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[GoldbergKarzanov2004](#ref-GoldbergKarzanov2004)]. The weighted problem has its own sequence of improvements: the scaling algorithm of Duan, Pettie, and Su finds an exact maximum-weight matching in $O(m\sqrt n\log(nW))$ time for integer weights of magnitude at most $W$ (Duan, Pettie, and Su 2018) <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[DuanPettieSu2018](#ref-DuanPettieSu2018)], with logarithms bounded below by a constant.

Algebraic methods offer another important approach. Random evaluation of the Tutte matrix connects matching to linear algebra (Lovász 1979) <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[Lovasz1979](#ref-Lovasz1979)]. Mucha and Sankowski, and subsequently Harvey, obtained randomized algorithms that construct maximum matchings at the matrix-multiplication time scale, conventionally written $O(n^\omega)$ field operations (Mucha and Sankowski 2004; Harvey 2009) <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[MuchaSankowski2004](#ref-MuchaSankowski2004), [Harvey2009](#ref-Harvey2009)]. These algorithms already recover explicit witnesses and can work over finite fields of polynomial size. Their matrix-multiplication bound is most competitive on dense graphs. Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> instead gives an almost-linear bound in the explicit input size throughout the range of densities, including $n^{2+o(1)}$ at the dense extreme.

For bipartite matching, the unit-capacity flow formulation permits continuous optimization to accelerate exact search. Mądry’s electrical-flow interior-point method (Mądry 2013) <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[Madry2013CentralPath](#ref-Madry2013CentralPath)] and the moderately dense algorithm of van den Brand et al. (Brand et al. 2020) <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[vanDenBrandEtAl2020BipartiteMatching](#ref-vanDenBrandEtAl2020BipartiteMatching)] were intermediate advances. The almost-linear exact integral-flow algorithms of Chen et al. (Chen et al. 2022) <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[FlowRandomized](#ref-FlowRandomized)] and van den Brand et al. (Brand et al. 2023) <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[FlowDeterministic](#ref-FlowDeterministic)] then yield almost-linear exact bipartite matching. General graphs require additional control of odd blocks. Here that control is provided by the matching state and its local projections. The deterministic flow theorem is used through the explicit static interface in Lemma <a href="#lem:integral-flow" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:integral-flow">2</a>.

The proof combines several established ideas with interfaces tailored to that state. Its constructive factor-critical blocks belong to the contraction-and-expansion tradition, while Hall’s theorem underlies the reassignment of their exposed vertices. The multiscale allocation and debt bounds are proved directly. The routing helper uses the cut-matching structure of Khandekar, Rao, and Vazirani (Khandekar, Rao, and Vazirani 2006) <a id="pm-cite-21"></a>[[KhandekarRaoVazirani2006](#ref-KhandekarRaoVazirani2006)], together with concentration and conductance estimates (Tropp 2012; Sinclair and Jerrum 1989) <a id="pm-cite-22"></a>[[TroppBernstein](#ref-TroppBernstein), [SinclairJerrum1989](#ref-SinclairJerrum1989)]. Its recursive virtual graphs follow the hierarchical random-walk embeddings of Ghaffari, Kuhn, and Su (Ghaffari, Kuhn, and Su 2017, sec. 3.1.2) <a id="pm-cite-23"></a>[[GhaffariKuhnSu2017](#ref-GhaffariKuhnSu2017)]. Here the sampled support and the substituted routes must also preserve supplied resource loads, with their full construction costs charged.

The use of approximate minimum-ratio circulations as optimization directions has its modern antecedent in the almost-linear flow framework (Chen et al. 2022; Brand et al. 2023) <a id="pm-cite-24"></a>[[FlowRandomized](#ref-FlowRandomized), [FlowDeterministic](#ref-FlowDeterministic)]. Chen et al. prove a deterministic *fully dynamic* approximate minimum-ratio-cycle theorem with flat forest representations (Chen et al. 2023, Definition 2.3 and Theorem 3.5) <a id="pm-cite-25"></a>[[IncrementalGraphs](#ref-IncrementalGraphs)]. Our routing hierarchy is related to the origin-aware construction of Rozhoň et al. (Rozhoň et al. 2022) <a id="pm-cite-26"></a>[[MinorAggregateRouting](#ref-MinorAggregateRouting)]; the finite forest family and associated cycle extraction adapt the construction in (Chen et al. 2023, sec. 4.5) <a id="pm-cite-27"></a>[[IncrementalGraphs](#ref-IncrementalGraphs)]. The preallocated forest copies used to lift recursive paths follow Kyng, Meierhans, and Probst Gutenberg (Kyng, Meierhans, and Probst Gutenberg 2023, sec. 3.3, Lemma 3.21 and Algorithm 5) <a id="pm-cite-28"></a>[[KyngMeierhansProbstGutenberg2023FlatForest](#ref-KyngMeierhansProbstGutenberg2023FlatForest)]. Sections <a href="#sec:engine" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:engine">5</a> and <a href="#sec:backend-updates" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:backend-updates">8</a> specify how retained mapped edges preserve accumulated coordinate changes and absolute motion through these representation updates. The recent distance-oracle approach of Kyng et al. (Kyng et al. 2026) <a id="pm-cite-29"></a>[[MinRatioDistanceOracles2026](#ref-MinRatioDistanceOracles2026)] provides another development of this method. Sections <a href="#sec:backend-quality" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:backend-quality">7</a> and <a href="#sec:backend-updates" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:backend-updates">8</a> supply the complete backend used here, including its adaptive guarantees, explicit changes of representation, stable edge-copy lifetimes, and bounds on traversal length before cancellation. Those guarantees are the inputs to our priced-step analysis.

<a id="pm-h-5"></a>
#### Main ideas and organization

The central subproblem is to find a perfect matching in a graph of maximum degree three. Section <a href="#sec:uniformization" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:uniformization">9</a> reduces exact cardinality queries to this subproblem while retaining the original edge identifiers, so that every successful answer can be decoded into a matching of the input graph.

The perfect-matching procedure maintains a current matching through a partition of the vertices. Its odd blocks, called *bins*, come with internal matchings after any prescribed vertex is left exposed. Some outside singleton vertices, called *rows*, are assigned along original edges to distinct bins. Each assigned bin matches its exposed vertex to its row; an unassigned bin contributes one unmatched vertex. The remaining vertices are stored as matched pairs, apart from unmatched vertices set aside during repairs. This representation permits a change of exposure without immediately expanding a nested block.

The difficulty is to repair this representation after structural changes without repeatedly scanning all its odd blocks. A *ledger* allocates a fixed amount from each row among its neighboring bins. The difference between a bin’s capacity and its allocated load is its residual. Several ledgers impose residual margins proportional to bin size; a nonnegative *debt* allows a margin to fail temporarily. At each settled state, the lowest ledger has no debt and certifies a strict Hall surplus for every nonempty set of bins. The highest ledger carries a logarithmic objective that must admit progress when a perfect matching exists and no structural operation is available.

The intermediate ledgers turn that progress into affordable repairs. A repair is an exact flow problem on row–bin incidences, initially explored only at bins whose debt is substantial relative to their size. An unexplored boundary bin temporarily accepts an optimistic amount of flow. If enough arrives, the algorithm explores that bin and permanently locks in an absorbed amount proportional to its size. The total available flow is bounded by the starting debt, so these locks bound the number of physical vertices examined. If the requested residual margin is infeasible, the repair instead removes a set of bins. The gap between adjacent margins makes this removal decrease debt in every preserved coarser ledger by an amount proportional to the removed size. Thus debt pays both for local exploration and for removing blocks from the state. The logarithmic optimization uses exact conservation throughout; integral cut inequalities turn a point whose total bound violation is below one into a certificate of exact feasibility. A final local cut also recovers the entire residual source-reachable set needed by the matching repair.

Both the matching objective and these local flow tests use the same *priced-step* interface. It searches for a circulation direction with sufficient objective increase relative to its weighted length. When it accepts such a direction, it takes a small exact dyadic step. The direction is represented by individual edges and paths in a changing forest, so a long path need not be expanded for every step. The implementation separately records signed coordinate changes and absolute motion. The latter counts repeated traversals before cancellation and controls when a coordinate must be read and repriced.

The cycle data structure supplying this interface is constructed in full. A static routing sparsifier first provides short routes whose total use of specified resources is bounded. During an interval of updates, a frozen collection of forests then decomposes every current circulation into local correction cycles and a circulation in one smaller graph. A hierarchy of routing snapshots limits the number of new representative edges added to that graph. Finally, prebuilt copies of the paths represented by its edges lift its forest back to the current graph. When recursive occurrences change at otherwise unaffected child labels, these copies retain their identities. This preserves the exact accumulated values used by the priced-step implementation; replacing an affected copy first transfers its saved history.

Let $S$ be a power-of-two upper bound on the number of vertices in the reduced graph. For each sufficiently large fixed parameter $a$, these ingredients yield time $S^{1+O(1/a)+o_a(1)}$ for a subcubic perfect-matching test. The coefficient hidden in $O(1/a)$ is absolute. Logarithmic powers may depend on the fixed parameter. Deterministic prerequisite failures suspend a parameter branch; exhausted random retries terminate its test with a possibly erroneous negative answer. This distinction permits an every-path asymptotic bound. Finally, instruction-level dovetailing with summable error budgets turns all fixed-parameter guarantees into Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>, without a convergence rate or an input-dependent table of thresholds.

Sections <a href="#sec:matching-state" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:matching-state">3</a> and <a href="#sec:projection-analysis" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:projection-analysis">4</a> develop the matching algorithm assuming the priced-step interface. Section <a href="#sec:engine" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:engine">5</a> proves that interface from the flat-cycle backend. Section <a href="#sec:routing-helper" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:routing-helper">6</a> gives the static helper, and Sections <a href="#sec:backend-quality" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:backend-quality">7</a> and <a href="#sec:backend-updates" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:backend-updates">8</a> prove the backend’s quality and implementation guarantees. Section <a href="#sec:uniformization" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:uniformization">9</a> completes the reductions and the uniform finite-word implementation. Section <a href="#sec:f-factors" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:f-factors">10</a> applies the resulting matching algorithm to prescribed-degree factors through a linearithmic-size reduction.

The principal interfaces and their proof dependencies are summarized below.

<div class="center">

| Result                                                                                               | Role in the proof                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
|:-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|:---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| Theorem <a href="#thm:subcubic" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:subcubic">20</a>       | Fixed-parameter subcubic perfect-matching search; Sections <a href="#sec:matching-state" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:matching-state">3</a>–<a href="#sec:projection-analysis" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:projection-analysis">4</a> use Lemma <a href="#lem:priced-steps" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:priced-steps">21</a>.           |
| Lemma <a href="#lem:priced-steps" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:priced-steps">21</a> | Priced logarithmic steps; Section <a href="#sec:engine" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:engine">5</a> proves the interface from Lemma <a href="#lem:flat-cycle" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:flat-cycle">22</a>.                                                                                                                                              |
| Lemma <a href="#lem:flat-cycle" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:flat-cycle">22</a>     | Flat-cycle backend; Sections <a href="#sec:backend-quality" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:backend-quality">7</a>–<a href="#sec:backend-updates" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:backend-updates">8</a> construct it using the static routing helper, Lemma <a href="#lem:routing-helper" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:routing-helper">25</a>. |
| Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>                | Explicit maximum matching with one input-size exponent envelope; Section <a href="#sec:uniformization" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:uniformization">9</a> applies the witness reductions and instruction-level schedule to Theorem <a href="#thm:subcubic" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:subcubic">20</a>.                                                  |

</div>

<a id="sec:preliminaries"></a>
### Conventions and static primitives

<a id="pm-h-7"></a>
#### Representations and fixed-parameter bounds

Auxiliary graphs may have parallel edges or arcs, always distinguished by identifiers. Fix a reference orientation on every variable edge. Traversing an edge backward negates its coefficient. A signed circulation has zero divergence at every vertex; its length norm for positive lengths $\ell$ is $$\|\ell f\|_1=\sum_e\ell_e|f_e|.$$ This norm differs from the *raw length* of a supplied traversal list: the latter counts every traversal with its multiplicity, before cancellation. Later a forest edge may map either to an actual graph edge, with its endpoint correspondence specified, or to a null edge whose two endpoints have the same image. A null traversal has zero actual projected length, although a reduction may assign it an additional positive bookkeeping length.

All representation sizes include vertices or slots, individual edges, terminal incidences, and explicitly stored lists. Ordinary adjacency lists, heaps, balanced dictionaries, and path-copy persistent maps incur logarithmic overhead per record operation. No argument permits a free scan of all coordinates after an implicit path update. Arithmetic and key-length costs are included separately when they exceed one word.

The matching procedure uses a master bound $S=2^s$, an integer parameter $a\ge1000$, and a reliability parameter $\sigma$. In the final schedule $\sigma\le a+1$. A bound of the form $$S^{O(1/a)+o_a(1)}$$ has an absolute coefficient in $O(1/a)$; for each fixed $a,\sigma$, the second exponent tends to zero as $S\to\infty$. Constants and powers of logarithms may depend on these fixed parameters. All smaller local routines measure their permitted logarithmic factors against the indicated master bound. The parameters are not fixed uniformly while taking $a\to\infty$.

An implementation may check a deterministic size or precision prerequisite. If that check is inadequate, it *suspends* the entire parameter branch without an answer. In contrast, exhaustion of a capped randomized routine is a *detected failure*; the calling perfect-matching test returns a negative answer promptly. The fixed-parameter analysis proves that deterministic suspensions cannot occur on any path once the input is sufficiently large for that fixed parameter. It does not require random attempts always to succeed. These conventions are used in Theorem <a href="#thm:subcubic" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:subcubic">20</a> and the schedule in Section <a href="#sec:uniformization" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:uniformization">9</a>.

<a id="pm-h-8"></a>
#### Exact integral flow

<a id="stmt-lem-integral-flow"></a>
<div id="lem:integral-flow" class="lemma">

**Lemma 2** (Static integral flow). *For an explicitly represented directed network of total size at most $P$, with integral capacities, demands and costs bounded in magnitude by a fixed power of $P$, exact integral minimum-cost flow can be computed uniformly and deterministically in $P^{1+o(1)}$ time. Its finite-precision operations can be simulated with polynomially logarithmic overhead on logarithmic-bit words. In particular, integral source–sink maximum flow with nonnegative upper capacities and zero lower capacities returns an explicit integral arc vector and a residual-reachability minimum-cut certificate within that bound.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-integral-flow"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-integral-flow</summary>

*Proof.* Use the deterministic minimum-cost-flow theorem of van den Brand et al. (Brand et al. 2023, Theorem 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-30"></a>[[FlowDeterministic](#ref-FlowDeterministic)], with its finite fixed-point model and deterministic integral rounding (Brand et al. 2023, sec. 2 and Lemma 4.1) <a id="pm-cite-31"></a>[[FlowDeterministic](#ref-FlowDeterministic)]. The stated logarithmic dependence on integral magnitudes and polynomial logarithmic word lengths is absorbed in $P^{o(1)}$. A fixed-point word is stored as a charged array of logarithmic-bit words. Longer virtual addresses can be keys in a dictionary of touched cells; no unused address space is initialized.

Maximum flow is obtained by adding a bounded sink-to-source return arc and minimizing its negative flow. Its capacity can be the sum of the original capacities. Feasibility and absence of a residual source–sink path certify maximality. Residual reachability supplies the cut. ◻

</details>

Only the nonnegative-upper-capacity maximum-flow specialization is called algorithmically below. Integral lower bounds are used in feasibility arguments through the classical cut criteria for circulation feasibility (Gale 1957; Ford and Fulkerson 1958) <a id="pm-cite-32"></a>[[Gale1957](#ref-Gale1957), [FordFulkerson1958](#ref-FordFulkerson1958)]. The following consequence records the precision margin we use.

<a id="stmt-prelim-small-violation"></a>
<div id="prelim:small-violation" class="lemma">

**Lemma 3** (Integral cuts from a small total violation). *Let every lower and upper arc bound be integral, with lower bound no larger than upper bound. If an exactly conserved real arc vector has total bound violation less than one, the bounds admit an exactly feasible integral circulation.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-prelim-small-violation"><summary>Proof of stmt-prelim-small-violation</summary>

*Proof.* For a vertex set $X$, feasibility requires the sum of lower bounds on arcs leaving $X$ to be at most the sum of upper bounds on arcs entering $X$. Conservation of the real vector implies that any failure of this inequality is at most its total bound violation. The two bound sums are integers, so a positive discrepancy would be at least one, a contradiction. The circulation cut criterion therefore holds. To obtain that criterion, subtract the lower bounds, introduce a super-source and super-sink for the resulting vertex imbalances, and apply the integral maximum-flow/minimum-cut theorem. Saturating the imbalance arcs gives the asserted integral circulation. ◻

</details>

Large capacities relative to a small local graph do not require a different finite program for each magnitude exponent.

<a id="stmt-lem-capacity-scaling"></a>
<div id="lem:capacity-scaling" class="lemma">

**Lemma 4** (One polynomial-capacity implementation). *For maximum flow with nonnegative integral upper capacities of at most $b$ bits, a single fixed polynomial-capacity version of Lemma <a href="#lem:integral-flow" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:integral-flow">2</a> suffices, using $b$ calls on linear-size residual graphs, together with the charged capacity arithmetic and identifier translations.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-capacity-scaling"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-capacity-scaling</summary>

*Proof.* Begin with zero capacities. In each binary digit round write the new capacity vector as $c'=2c+\xi$, where every $\xi_e$ is zero or one. If $f$ was a maximum flow of value $v$ for $c$, then $2f$ is feasible for $c'$. Let $A$ be the number of original arcs. An old minimum cut has new capacity at most $2v+A$, so the largest residual augmentation has integral value $\Delta\le A$.

Decompose an optimal integral residual augmentation into source–sink paths and cycles and discard the cycles. Its remaining total path value is $\Delta$, so every forward and reverse residual arc carries at most $\Delta\le A$. Truncating every residual capacity at $A$ therefore preserves the optimum augmentation. Compute it with the fixed polynomial-capacity routine. Adding forward usage and subtracting reverse usage updates $2f$ to a maximum flow for $c'$. This proves the induction over digits.

Local vertices and arcs can be renumbered, using dictionaries to translate their caller identifiers on input and output. Integer arrays, comparisons, and translations are charged at their actual bit or multiword cost. In the fixed-parameter applications below, $b$ and all key lengths contribute only the permitted logarithmic powers in the master size. ◻

</details>
